    10	/// A trait that provide the timeout related function for [`Waiter`] and [`WaitQueue`]`.
    11	pub trait WaitTimeout {
    12	    /// Waits until some condition returns `Some(_)` or a given timeout is reached.
    13	    ///
    14	    /// # Errors
    15	    ///
    16	    /// If the condition does not become `Some(_)` before the timeout is reached, this function
    17	    /// will return an error with [`ETIME`].
    18	    ///
    19	    /// [`ETIME`]: crate::error::Errno::ETIME
    20	    #[track_caller]
    21	    fn wait_until_or_timeout<'a, F, T, R>(&self, mut cond: F, timeout: T) -> Result<R>
    22	    where
    23	        F: FnMut() -> Option<R>,
    24	        T: Into<TimeoutExt<'a>>,
    25	    {
    26	        let timeout = timeout.into();
    27	        let timeout_inner = match timeout.check_expired() {
    28	            Ok(inner) => inner,
    29	            Err(err) => return cond().ok_or(err),
    30	        };
    31	
    32	        self.wait_until_or_timeout_cancelled(cond, || Ok(()), timeout_inner)
    33	    }
    34	
    35	    /// Waits until some condition returns `Some(_)` or is cancelled by the timeout or cancel
    36	    /// condition.
    37	    ///
    38	    /// # Errors
    39	    ///
    40	    /// If the condition does not become `Some(_)` before the cancellation, this function
    41	    /// will return:
    42	    ///  - an error with [`ETIME`] if the timeout is reached;
    43	    ///  - the error returned by the cancel condition if the cancel condition returns `Err(_)`.
    44	    #[doc(hidden)]
    45	    #[track_caller]
    46	    fn wait_until_or_timeout_cancelled<F, R, FCancel>(
    47	        &self,
    48	        cond: F,
    49	        cancel_cond: FCancel,
    50	        timeout: Option<&ManagedTimeout>,
    51	    ) -> Result<R>
    52	    where
    53	        F: FnMut() -> Option<R>,
    54	        FCancel: Fn() -> Result<()>;
    55	}
    56	
    57	/// A timeout with extended semantics.
    58	pub enum TimeoutExt<'a> {
    59	    /// The timeout will never fire.
    60	    Never,
    61	    /// The timeout will expire later according to [`ManagedTimeout`].
    62	    At(ManagedTimeout<'a>),
    63	}
    64	
    65	impl<'a> TimeoutExt<'a> {
    66	    /// Checks whether the timeout is expired.
    67	    ///
    68	    /// This method will return:
    69	    ///  - `Ok(Some(_))` if the timeout isn't expired but it may be expired later.
    70	    ///  - `Ok(None)` if the timeout will never be expired.
    71	    ///  - `Err(ETIME)` if the timeout is expired.
    72	    pub fn check_expired(&self) -> Result<Option<&ManagedTimeout<'a>>> {
    73	        match self {
    74	            TimeoutExt::At(inner) if inner.is_expired() => {
    75	                return_errno_with_message!(Errno::ETIME, "the time limit is reached")
    76	            }
    77	            TimeoutExt::At(inner) => Ok(Some(inner)),
    78	            TimeoutExt::Never => Ok(None),
    79	        }
    80	    }
    81	
    82	    /// Freezes the expired time.
    83	    ///
    84	    /// This works in the same way as [`ManagedTimeout::freeze`].
    85	    pub fn freeze(&mut self) {
    86	        match self {
    87	            Self::Never => (),
    88	            Self::At(timeout) => timeout.freeze(),
    89	        }
    90	    }
    91	}
    92	
    93	impl From<&Duration> for TimeoutExt<'_> {
    94	    fn from(value: &Duration) -> Self {
    95	        Self::At(ManagedTimeout::new(*value))
    96	    }
    97	}
    98	
    99	impl From<Option<&Duration>> for TimeoutExt<'_> {
   100	    fn from(value: Option<&Duration>) -> Self {
   101	        match value {
   102	            Some(duration) => duration.into(),
   103	            None => Self::Never,
   104	        }
   105	    }
   106	}
   107	
   108	impl<'a> From<ManagedTimeout<'a>> for TimeoutExt<'a> {
   109	    fn from(value: ManagedTimeout<'a>) -> Self {
   110	        Self::At(value)
   111	    }
   112	}
   113	
   114	impl<'a> From<Option<ManagedTimeout<'a>>> for TimeoutExt<'a> {
   115	    fn from(value: Option<ManagedTimeout<'a>>) -> Self {
   116	        match value {
   117	            Some(timeout) => timeout.into(),
   118	            None => Self::Never,
   119	        }
   120	    }
   121	}
   122	
   123	/// A [`Timeout`] with the associated [`TimerManager`].
   124	pub struct ManagedTimeout<'a> {
   125	    timeout: Timeout,
   126	    manager: &'a Arc<TimerManager>,
   127	}
   128	
   129	impl<'a> ManagedTimeout<'a> {
   130	    /// Creates a new `ManagedTimeout` with the JIFFIES timer manager.
   131	    pub fn new(timeout: Duration) -> Self {
   132	        let timeout = Timeout::After(timeout);
   133	        let manager = JIFFIES_TIMER_MANAGER.get().unwrap();
   134	        Self::new_with_manager(timeout, manager)
   135	    }
   136	
   137	    /// Creates a new `ManagedTimeout` with the given timer manager.
   138	    pub const fn new_with_manager(timeout: Timeout, manager: &'a Arc<TimerManager>) -> Self {
   139	        Self { timeout, manager }
   140	    }
   141	
   142	    /// Returns weather the timeout is expired.
   143	    pub fn is_expired(&self) -> bool {
   144	        self.manager.is_expired_timeout(&self.timeout)
   145	    }
   146	
   147	    /// Freezes the expired time.
   148	    ///
   149	    /// If the timeout is specified as an instant after a period of time from the current time
   150	    /// (i.e., [`Timeout::After`]), this method will freeze the timeout by converting it to a fixed
   151	    /// instant (i.e., [`Timeout::When`]).
   152	    pub fn freeze(&mut self) {
   153	        self.timeout = match self.timeout {
   154	            Timeout::When(instant) => Timeout::When(instant),
   155	            Timeout::After(duration) => Timeout::When(self.manager.clock().read_time() + duration),
   156	        }
   157	    }
   158	
   159	    /// Creates a timer for the timeout.
   160	    pub fn create_timer<F>(&self, callback: F) -> Arc<Timer>
   161	    where
   162	        F: Fn() + Send + Sync + 'static,
   163	    {
   164	        let timer = self.manager.create_timer(callback);
   165	        timer.set_timeout(self.timeout.clone());
   166	        timer
   167	    }
   168	}
   169	
   170	impl WaitTimeout for Waiter {
   171	    fn wait_until_or_timeout_cancelled<F, R, FCancel>(
   172	        &self,
   173	        cond: F,
   174	        cancel_cond: FCancel,
   175	        timeout: Option<&ManagedTimeout>,
   176	    ) -> Result<R>
   177	    where
   178	        F: FnMut() -> Option<R>,
   179	        FCancel: Fn() -> Result<()>,
   180	    {
   181	        // No fast paths for `Waiter`. If the caller wants a fast path, it should do so _before_
   182	        // the waiter is created.
   183	
   184	        let timer = timeout.map(|timeout| {
   185	            let waker = self.waker();
   186	            timeout.create_timer(move || {
   187	                waker.wake_up();
   188	            })
   189	        });
   190	
   191	        let cancel_cond = {
   192	            let timer = timer.clone();
   193	
   194	            move || {
   195	                if timer
   196	                    .as_ref()
   197	                    .is_some_and(|timer| timer.remain() == Duration::ZERO)
   198	                {
   199	                    return_errno_with_message!(Errno::ETIME, "the time limit is reached");
   200	                }
   201	
   202	                cancel_cond()
   203	            }
   204	        };
   205	
   206	        let res = self.wait_until_or_cancelled(cond, cancel_cond);
   207	
   208	        // If `res` is not `ETIME` error, then the timeout may not have been expired.
   209	        // We cancel it manually.
   210	        if let Some(timer) = timer
   211	            && !res
   212	                .as_ref()
   213	                .is_err_and(|e: &Error| e.error() == Errno::ETIME)
   214	        {
   215	            timer.cancel();
   216	        }
   217	
   218	        res
   219	    }
   220	}
   221	
   222	impl WaitTimeout for WaitQueue {
   223	    fn wait_until_or_timeout_cancelled<F, R, FCancel>(
   224	        &self,
   225	        mut cond: F,
   226	        cancel_cond: FCancel,
   227	        timeout: Option<&ManagedTimeout>,
   228	    ) -> Result<R>
   229	    where
   230	        F: FnMut() -> Option<R>,
   231	        FCancel: Fn() -> Result<()>,
   232	    {
   233	        // Fast path:
   234	        if let Some(res) = cond() {
   235	            return Ok(res);
   236	        }
   237	
   238	        let (waiter, _) = Waiter::new_pair();
   239	        let cond = || {
   240	            self.enqueue(waiter.waker());
   241	            cond()
   242	        };
   243	        waiter.wait_until_or_timeout_cancelled(cond, cancel_cond, timeout)
   244	    }
   245	}
   150	}

   152	impl Default for WaitQueue {
   153	    fn default() -> Self {
   154	        Self::new()
   155	    }
   156	}
   157	
   158	/// A waiter that can put the current thread to sleep until it is woken up by the associated
   159	/// [`Waker`].
   160	///
   161	/// By definition, a waiter belongs to the current thread, so it cannot be sent to another thread
   162	/// and its reference cannot be shared between threads.
   163	pub struct Waiter {
   164	    waker: Arc<Waker>,
   165	}
   166	
   167	impl !Send for Waiter {}
   168	impl !Sync for Waiter {}
   169	
   170	/// A waker that can wake up the associated [`Waiter`].
   171	///
   172	/// A waker can be created by calling [`Waiter::new_pair`]. This method creates an `Arc<Waker>` that can
   173	/// be used across different threads.
   174	pub struct Waker {
   175	    has_woken: AtomicBool,
   176	    task: Arc<Task>,
   177	}
   178	
   179	impl Waiter {
   180	    /// Creates a waiter and its associated [`Waker`].
   181	    pub fn new_pair() -> (Self, Arc<Waker>) {
   182	        let waker = Arc::new(Waker {
   183	            has_woken: AtomicBool::new(false),
   184	            task: Task::current().unwrap().cloned(),
   185	        });
   186	        let waiter = Self {
   187	            waker: waker.clone(),
   188	        };
   189	        (waiter, waker)
   190	    }
   191	
   192	    /// Waits until the waiter is woken up by calling [`Waker::wake_up`] on the associated
   193	    /// [`Waker`].
   194	    ///
   195	    /// This method returns immediately if the waiter has been woken since the end of the last call
   196	    /// to this method (or since the waiter was created, if this method has not been called
   197	    /// before). Otherwise, it puts the current thread to sleep until the waiter is woken up.
   198	    #[track_caller]
   199	    pub fn wait(&self) {
   200	        self.waker.do_wait();
   201	    }
   202	
   203	    /// Waits until some condition is met or the cancel condition becomes true.
   204	    ///
   205	    /// This method will return `Ok(_)` if the condition returns `Some(_)`, and will stop waiting
   206	    /// if the cancel condition returns `Err(_)`. In this situation, this method will return the `Err(_)`
   207	    /// generated by the cancel condition.
   208	    #[track_caller]
   209	    pub fn wait_until_or_cancelled<F, R, FCancel, E>(
   210	        &self,
   211	        mut cond: F,
   212	        cancel_cond: FCancel,
   213	    ) -> core::result::Result<R, E>
   214	    where
   215	        F: FnMut() -> Option<R>,
   216	        FCancel: Fn() -> core::result::Result<(), E>,
   217	    {
   218	        loop {
   219	            if let Some(res) = cond() {
   220	                return Ok(res);
   221	            };
   222	
   223	            if let Err(e) = cancel_cond() {
   224	                // Close the waker and check again to avoid missing a wake event.
   225	                self.waker.close();
   226	                return cond().ok_or(e);
   227	            }
   228	
   229	            self.wait();
   230	        }
   231	    }
   232	
   233	    /// Gets the associated [`Waker`] of the current waiter.
   234	    pub fn waker(&self) -> Arc<Waker> {
   235	        self.waker.clone()
   236	    }
   237	
   238	    /// Returns the task that the associated waker will attempt to wake up.
   239	    pub fn task(&self) -> &Arc<Task> {
   240	        &self.waker.task
   241	    }
   242	}
   243	
   244	impl Drop for Waiter {
   245	    fn drop(&mut self) {
   246	        // When dropping the waiter, we need to close the waker to ensure that if someone wants to
   247	        // wake up the waiter afterwards, they will perform a no-op.
   248	        self.waker.close();
   249	    }
   250	}
   251	
   252	impl Waker {
   253	    /// Wakes up the associated [`Waiter`].
   254	    ///
   255	    /// This method returns `true` if the waiter is woken by this call. It returns `false` if the
   256	    /// waiter has already been woken by a previous call to the method, or if the waiter has been
   257	    /// dropped.
   258	    ///
   259	    /// Note that if this method returns `true`, it implies that the wake event will be properly
   260	    /// delivered, _or_ that the waiter will be dropped after being woken. It's up to the caller to
   261	    /// handle the latter case properly to avoid missing the wake event.
   262	    pub fn wake_up(&self) -> bool {
   263	        if self.has_woken.swap(true, Ordering::Release) {
   264	            return false;
   265	        }
   266	        scheduler::unpark_target(self.task.clone());
   267	
   268	        true
   269	    }
   270	
   271	    #[track_caller]
   272	    fn do_wait(&self) {
   273	        while !self.has_woken.swap(false, Ordering::Acquire) {
   274	            scheduler::park_current(|| self.has_woken.load(Ordering::Acquire));
   275	        }
   276	    }
   277	
   278	    fn close(&self) {
   279	        // This must use `Ordering::Acquire`, although we do not care about the return value. See
   280	        // the memory order explanation at the top of the file for details.

 succeeded in 160ms:

     8	pub fn sys_pause(_ctx: &Context) -> Result<SyscallReturn> {
     9	    // FIXME: like sleep, paused thread can only be interrupted by signals that will call signal
    10	    // handler or terminate current process
    11	    let waiter = Waiter::new_pair().0;
    12	
    13	    waiter.pause_until(|| None)?;
    14	
    15	    unreachable!("[Internal Error] pause should always return EINTR");
    16	}

     3	use ostd::sync::Waiter;
     4	
     5	use super::SyscallReturn;
     6	use crate::{
     7	    prelude::*,
     8	    process::signal::{
     9	        constants::{SIGKILL, SIGSTOP},
    10	        sig_mask::SigMask,
    11	        with_sigmask_changed,
    12	    },

    14	
    15	pub fn sys_rt_sigsuspend(
    16	    sigmask_addr: Vaddr,
    17	    sigmask_size: usize,
    18	    ctx: &Context,
    19	) -> Result<SyscallReturn> {
    20	    debug!(
    21	        "sigmask_addr = 0x{:x}, sigmask_size = {}",
    22	        sigmask_addr, sigmask_size
    23	    );
    24	
    25	    if sigmask_size != core::mem::size_of::<SigMask>() {
    26	        return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EINVAL, "invalid sigmask size");
    27	    }
    28	
    29	    let sigmask = {
    30	        let mut mask: SigMask = ctx.user_space().read_val(sigmask_addr)?;
    31	        // It is not possible to block SIGKILL or SIGSTOP,
    32	        // specifying these signals in mask has no effect.
    33	        mask -= SIGKILL;
    34	        mask -= SIGSTOP;
    35	        mask
    36	    };
    37	
    38	    // Wait until receiving any signal
    39	    let waiter = Waiter::new_pair().0;
    40	    with_sigmask_changed(
    41	        ctx,
    42	        |old_mask| old_mask + sigmask,
    43	        || waiter.pause_until(|| None::<()>),
    44	    )?;
    45	
    46	    // This syscall should always return `Err(EINTR)`. This path should never be reached.
    47	    unreachable!("rt_sigsuspend always return EINTR");
    48	}

     6	use crate::{
     7	    fs::file_table::FileDesc,

    13	pub fn sys_pselect6(
    14	    nfds: FileDesc,
    15	    readfds_addr: Vaddr,
    16	    writefds_addr: Vaddr,
    17	    exceptfds_addr: Vaddr,
    18	    timespec_addr: Vaddr,
    19	    sigmask_addr: Vaddr,
    20	    ctx: &Context,
    21	) -> Result<SyscallReturn> {
    22	    let user_space = ctx.user_space();
    23	
    24	    let timeout = if timespec_addr != 0 {
    25	        let time_spec = user_space.read_val::<timespec_t>(timespec_addr)?;
    26	        Some(Duration::try_from(time_spec)?)
    27	    } else {
    28	        None
    29	    };
    30	
    31	    let operate = || {
    32	        do_sys_select(
    33	            nfds,
    34	            readfds_addr,
    35	            writefds_addr,
    36	            exceptfds_addr,
    37	            timeout,
    38	            ctx,
    39	        )
    40	    };
    41	
    42	    if sigmask_addr != 0 {
    43	        let sigmask_with_size = user_space.read_val::<SigMaskWithSize>(sigmask_addr)?;
    44	        if !sigmask_with_size.is_valid() {
    45	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EINVAL, "sigmask size is invalid")
    46	        }
    47	        with_sigmask_changed(ctx, |_: SigMask| sigmask_with_size.sigmask, operate)
    48	    } else {
    49	        operate()
    50	    }
    51	}
    52	
    53	#[repr(C)]
    54	#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, Pod)]
    55	struct SigMaskWithSize {
    56	    sigmask: SigMask,
    57	    sigmasksize: usize,
    58	}
    59	
    60	impl SigMaskWithSize {
    61	    const fn is_valid(&self) -> bool {
    62	        self.sigmask.is_empty() || self.sigmasksize == size_of::<SigMask>()
    63	    }
    64	}

    12	pub fn sys_ppoll(
    13	    fds: Vaddr,
    14	    nfds: u32,
    15	    timespec_addr: Vaddr,
    16	    sigmask_addr: Vaddr,
    17	    sigmask_size: usize,
    18	    ctx: &Context,
    19	) -> Result<SyscallReturn> {
    20	    let user_space = ctx.user_space();
    21	
    22	    let timeout = if timespec_addr != 0 {
    23	        let time_spec = user_space.read_val::<timespec_t>(timespec_addr)?;
    24	        Some(Duration::try_from(time_spec)?)
    25	    } else {
    26	        None
    27	    };
    28	
    29	    if sigmask_addr != 0 {
    30	        if sigmask_size != size_of::<SigMask>() {
    31	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EINVAL, "invalid sigmask size");
    32	        }
    33	
    34	        let sigmask = user_space.read_val::<SigMask>(sigmask_addr)?;
    35	        with_sigmask_changed(ctx, |_| sigmask, || do_sys_poll(fds, nfds, timeout, ctx))
    36	    } else {
    37	        do_sys_poll(fds, nfds, timeout, ctx)
    38	    }
    39	
    40	    // TODO: Write back the remaining time to `timespec_addr`.
    41	    //
    42	    // The ppoll system call should write back the remaining time,
    43	    // yet the function counterpart in libc hides this behavior to
    44	    // make the API more portable across different UNIX-like OSes.
    45	    // For the maximized Linux compatibility, we should follow Linux's behavior.
    46	    // But this cannot be readily achieved given how our internal synchronization primitives
    47	    // such as `Pause` and `WaitTimeout` work.
    48	}

     3	use core::time::Duration;

     5	use ostd::sync::Waiter;

     7	use super::{clock_gettime::read_clock, ClockId, SyscallReturn};
     8	use crate::{
     9	    prelude::*,
    10	    time::{
    11	        clockid_t,
    12	        clocks::{BootTimeClock, MonotonicClock, RealTimeClock},
    13	        timer::Timeout,
    14	        timespec_t,
    15	        wait::ManagedTimeout,
    16	        TIMER_ABSTIME,
    17	    },

    20	pub fn sys_nanosleep(
    21	    request_timespec_addr: Vaddr,
    22	    remain_timespec_addr: Vaddr,
    23	    ctx: &Context,
    24	) -> Result<SyscallReturn> {
    25	    let clockid = ClockId::CLOCK_MONOTONIC;
    26	
    27	    do_clock_nanosleep(
    28	        clockid as clockid_t,
    29	        false,
    30	        request_timespec_addr,
    31	        remain_timespec_addr,
    32	        ctx,
    33	    )
    34	}
    35	
    36	pub fn sys_clock_nanosleep(
    37	    clockid: clockid_t,
    38	    flags: i32,
    39	    request_timespec_addr: Vaddr,
    40	    remain_timespec_addr: Vaddr,
    41	    ctx: &Context,
    42	) -> Result<SyscallReturn> {
    43	    let is_abs_time = if flags == 0 {
    44	        false
    45	    } else if flags == TIMER_ABSTIME {
    46	        true
    47	    } else {
    48	        unreachable!()
    49	    };
    50	
    51	    do_clock_nanosleep(
    52	        clockid,
    53	        is_abs_time,
    54	        request_timespec_addr,
    55	        remain_timespec_addr,
    56	        ctx,
    57	    )
    58	}
    59	
    60	fn do_clock_nanosleep(
    61	    clockid: clockid_t,
    62	    is_abs_time: bool,
    63	    request_timespec_addr: Vaddr,
    64	    remain_timespec_addr: Vaddr,
    65	    ctx: &Context,
    66	) -> Result<SyscallReturn> {
    67	    let request_time = {
    68	        let timespec = ctx
    69	            .user_space()
    70	            .read_val::<timespec_t>(request_timespec_addr)?;
    71	        Duration::try_from(timespec)?
    72	    };
    73	
    74	    debug!(
    75	        "clockid = {:?}, is_abs_time = {}, request_time = {:?}, remain_timespec_addr = 0x{:x}",
    76	        clockid, is_abs_time, request_time, remain_timespec_addr
    77	    );
    78	
    79	    let start_time = read_clock(clockid, ctx)?;
    80	    let duration = if is_abs_time {
    81	        if request_time < start_time {
    82	            return Ok(SyscallReturn::Return(0));
    83	        }
    84	
    85	        request_time - start_time
    86	    } else {
    87	        request_time
    88	    };
    89	
    90	    // FIXME: sleeping thread can only be interrupted by signals that will call signal handler or terminate
    91	    // current process. i.e., the signals that should be ignored will not interrupt sleeping thread.
    92	    let waiter = Waiter::new_pair().0;
    93	
    94	    let timer_manager = {
    95	        let clock_id = ClockId::try_from(clockid)?;
    96	        match clock_id {
    97	            ClockId::CLOCK_BOOTTIME => BootTimeClock::timer_manager(),
    98	            ClockId::CLOCK_MONOTONIC => MonotonicClock::timer_manager(),
    99	            ClockId::CLOCK_REALTIME => RealTimeClock::timer_manager(),
   100	            // FIXME: We should better not expose this prof timer manager.
   101	            ClockId::CLOCK_PROCESS_CPUTIME_ID => {
   102	                ctx.process.timer_manager().prof_timer().timer_manager()
   103	            }
   104	            // FIXME: From the manual,
   105	            // the CPU clock IDs returned by clock_getcpuclockid(3)
   106	            // and pthread_getcpuclockid(3) can also be passed in clockid.
   107	            // But it's not covered here.
   108	            _ => return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EINVAL, "unknown clockid for clock_nanosleep"),
   109	        }
   110	    };
   111	
   112	    let res = waiter.pause_until_or_timeout(
   113	        || None,
   114	        ManagedTimeout::new_with_manager(Timeout::After(duration), timer_manager),
   115	    );
   116	
   117	    match res {
   118	        Err(e) if e.error() == Errno::ETIME => Ok(SyscallReturn::Return(0)),
   119	        Err(e) if e.error() == Errno::EINTR => {
   120	            let end_time = read_clock(clockid, ctx)?;
   121	
   122	            if end_time >= start_time + duration {
   123	                return Ok(SyscallReturn::Return(0));
   124	            }
   125	
   126	            if remain_timespec_addr != 0 && !is_abs_time {
   127	                let remaining_duration = (start_time + duration) - end_time;
   128	                let remaining_timespec = timespec_t::from(remaining_duration);
   129	                ctx.user_space()
   130	                    .write_val(remain_timespec_addr, &remaining_timespec)?;
   131	            }
   132	
   133	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EINTR, "sleep was interrupted");
   134	        }
   135	        Ok(()) | Err(_) => unreachable!(),
   136	    }
   137	}

   162	impl UnixStreamSocket {
   163	    pub fn new(is_nonblocking: bool, is_seqpacket: bool) -> Arc<Self> {
   164	        Self::new_init(Init::new(), is_nonblocking, is_seqpacket)
   165	    }
   166	
   167	    pub fn new_pair(is_nonblocking: bool, is_seqpacket: bool) -> (Arc<Self>, Arc<Self>) {
   168	        let cred = SocketCred::<ReadDupOp>::new_current();
   169	        let options = OptionSet::new();
   170	
   171	        let (conn_a, conn_b) = Connected::new_pair(
   172	            None,
   173	            None,
   174	            EndpointState::default(),
   175	            EndpointState::default(),
   176	            cred.dup().restrict(),
   177	            cred.restrict(),
   178	            &options.socket,
   179	        );
   180	        (
   181	            Self::new_connected(conn_a, options, is_nonblocking, is_seqpacket),
   182	            Self::new_connected(conn_b, OptionSet::new(), is_nonblocking, is_seqpacket),
   183	        )
   184	    }
   185	
   186	    fn try_send(
   187	        &self,
   188	        buf: &mut dyn MultiRead,
   189	        aux_data: &mut AuxiliaryData,
   190	        _flags: SendRecvFlags,
   191	    ) -> Result<usize> {
   192	        match self.state.read().as_ref() {
   193	            State::Connected(connected) => connected.try_write(buf, aux_data, self.is_seqpacket),
   194	            State::Init(_) | State::Listen(_) => {
   195	                return_errno_with_message!(Errno::ENOTCONN, "the socket is not connected")
   196	            }
   197	        }
   198	    }
   199	
   200	    fn try_recv(
   201	        &self,
   202	        buf: &mut dyn MultiWrite,
   203	        _flags: SendRecvFlags,
   204	    ) -> Result<(usize, Vec<ControlMessage>)> {
   205	        match self.state.read().as_ref() {
   206	            State::Connected(connected) => connected.try_read(buf, self.is_seqpacket),
   207	            State::Init(_) | State::Listen(_) => {
   208	                return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EINVAL, "the socket is not connected")
   209	            }
   210	        }
   211	    }
   212	
   213	    fn try_connect(&self, backlog: &Arc<Backlog>) -> Result<()> {
   214	        let mut state = self.state.write();
   215	        let options = self.options.read();
   216	
   217	        state.borrow_result(|owned_state| {
   218	            let init = match owned_state {
   219	                State::Init(init) => init,
   220	                State::Listen(listener) => {
   221	                    return (
   222	                        State::Listen(listener),
   223	                        Err(Error::with_message(
   224	                            Errno::EINVAL,
   225	                            "the socket is listening",
   226	                        )),
   227	                    );
   228	                }
   229	                State::Connected(connected) => {
   230	                    return (
   231	                        State::Connected(connected),
   232	                        Err(Error::with_message(
   233	                            Errno::EISCONN,
   234	                            "the socket is connected",
   235	                        )),
   236	                    );
   237	                }
   238	            };
   239	
   240	            let connected = match backlog.push_incoming(
   241	                init,
   242	                self.pollee.clone(),
   243	                &options.socket,
   244	                self.is_seqpacket,
   245	            ) {
   246	                Ok(connected) => connected,
   247	                Err((err, init)) => return (State::Init(init), Err(err)),
   248	            };
   249	
   250	            (State::Connected(connected), Ok(()))
   251	        })
   252	    }
   253	
   254	    fn try_accept(&self) -> Result<(Arc<dyn FileLike>, SocketAddr)> {
   255	        match self.state.read().as_ref() {
   256	            State::Listen(listen) => listen.try_accept(self.is_seqpacket) as _,
   257	            State::Init(_) | State::Connected(_) => {
   258	                return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EINVAL, "the socket is not listening")
   259	            }
   260	        }
   261	    }
   262	}

   270	        self.pollee
   271	            .poll_with(mask, poller, || self.state.read().check_io_events())
   272	    }

   276	    fn is_nonblocking(&self) -> bool {
   277	        self.is_nonblocking.load(Ordering::Relaxed)
   278	    }
   279	
   280	    fn set_nonblocking(&self, nonblocking: bool) {
   281	        self.is_nonblocking.store(nonblocking, Ordering::Relaxed);
   282	    }
   283	}
   284	
   285	impl Socket for UnixStreamSocket {
   286	    fn bind(&self, socket_addr: SocketAddr) -> Result<()> {
   287	        let addr = UnixSocketAddr::try_from(socket_addr)?;
   288	
   289	        match self.state.write().as_mut() {
   290	            State::Init(init) => init.bind(addr),
   291	            State::Connected(connected) => connected.bind(addr),
   292	            State::Listen(_) => {
   293	                // Listening sockets are always already bound.
   294	                addr.bind_unnamed()
   295	            }
   296	        }
   297	    }
   298	
   299	    fn connect(&self, socket_addr: SocketAddr) -> Result<()> {
   300	        let remote_addr = UnixSocketAddr::try_from(socket_addr)?.connect()?;
   301	        let backlog = get_backlog(&remote_addr)?;
   302	
   303	        if self.is_nonblocking() {
   304	            self.try_connect(&backlog)
   305	        } else {
   306	            backlog.pause_until(|| self.try_connect(&backlog))
   307	        }
   308	    }
   309	
   310	    fn listen(&self, backlog: usize) -> Result<()> {
   311	        const SOMAXCONN: usize = 4096;
   312	
   313	        // Linux allows a maximum of `backlog + 1` sockets in the backlog queue. Although this
   314	        // seems to be mostly an implementation detail, we follow the exact Linux behavior to
   315	        // ensure that our regression tests pass with the Linux kernel.
   316	        let backlog = backlog.saturating_add(1).min(SOMAXCONN);
   317	
   318	        let mut state = self.state.write();
   319	
   320	        state.borrow_result(|owned_state| {
   321	            let init = match owned_state {
   322	                State::Init(init) => init,
   323	                State::Listen(listener) => {
   324	                    listener.listen(backlog);
   325	                    return (State::Listen(listener), Ok(()));
   326	                }
   327	                State::Connected(connected) => {
   328	                    return (
   329	                        State::Connected(connected),
   330	                        Err(Error::with_message(
   331	                            Errno::EINVAL,
   332	                            "the socket is connected",
   333	                        )),
   334	                    );
   335	                }
   336	            };
   337	
   338	            let listener = match init.listen(backlog, self.pollee.clone(), self.is_seqpacket) {
   339	                Ok(listener) => listener,
   340	                Err((err, init)) => {
   341	                    return (State::Init(init), Err(err));
   342	                }
   343	            };
   344	
   345	            (State::Listen(listener), Ok(()))
   346	        })
   347	    }
   348	
   349	    fn accept(&self) -> Result<(Arc<dyn FileLike>, SocketAddr)> {
   350	        self.block_on(IoEvents::IN, || self.try_accept())
   351	    }
   352	
   353	    fn shutdown(&self, cmd: SockShutdownCmd) -> Result<()> {
   354	        match self.state.read().as_ref() {
   355	            State::Init(init) => init.shutdown(cmd, &self.pollee),
   356	            State::Listen(listen) => listen.shutdown(cmd, &self.pollee),
   357	            State::Connected(connected) => connected.shutdown(cmd),
   358	        }
   359	
   360	        Ok(())
   361	    }
   362	
   363	    fn addr(&self) -> Result<SocketAddr> {
   364	        let addr = match self.state.read().as_ref() {
   365	            State::Init(init) => init.addr().cloned(),
   366	            State::Listen(listen) => Some(listen.addr().clone()),
   367	            State::Connected(connected) => connected.addr(),
   368	        };
   369	
   370	        Ok(addr.into())
   371	    }
   372	
   373	    fn peer_addr(&self) -> Result<SocketAddr> {
   374	        let peer_addr = match self.state.read().as_ref() {
   375	            State::Connected(connected) => connected.peer_addr(),
   376	            State::Init(_) | State::Listen(_) => {
   377	                return_errno_with_message!(Errno::ENOTCONN, "the socket is not connected")
   378	            }
   379	        };
   380	
   381	        Ok(peer_addr.into())
   382	    }
   383	
   384	    fn sendmsg(
   385	        &self,
   386	        reader: &mut dyn MultiRead,
   387	        message_header: MessageHeader,
   388	        flags: SendRecvFlags,
   389	    ) -> Result<usize> {
   390	        // TODO: Deal with flags
   391	        if !flags.is_all_supported() {
   392	            warn!("unsupported flags: {:?}", flags);
   393	        }
   394	
   395	        let MessageHeader {
   396	            control_messages,
   397	            addr,
   398	        } = message_header;
   399	
   400	        // According to the Linux man pages, `EISCONN` _may_ be returned when the destination
   401	        // address is specified for a connection-mode socket. In practice, `sendmsg` on UNIX stream
   402	        // sockets will fail due to that. We follow the same behavior as the Linux implementation.
   403	        if !self.is_seqpacket && addr.is_some() {
   404	            match self.state.read().as_ref() {
   405	                State::Init(_) | State::Listen(_) => return_errno_with_message!(
   406	                    Errno::EOPNOTSUPP,
   407	                    "sending to a specific address is not allowed on UNIX stream sockets"
   408	                ),
   409	                State::Connected(_) => return_errno_with_message!(
   410	                    Errno::EISCONN,
   411	                    "sending to a specific address is not allowed on UNIX stream sockets"
   412	                ),
   413	            }
   414	        }
   415	        let mut auxiliary_data = AuxiliaryData::from_control(control_messages)?;
   416	
   417	        self.block_on(IoEvents::OUT, || {
   418	            self.try_send(reader, &mut auxiliary_data, flags)
   419	        })
   420	    }
   421	
   422	    fn recvmsg(
   423	        &self,
   424	        writer: &mut dyn MultiWrite,
   425	        flags: SendRecvFlags,
   426	    ) -> Result<(usize, MessageHeader)> {
   427	        // TODO: Deal with flags
   428	        if !flags.is_all_supported() {
   429	            warn!("unsupported flags: {:?}", flags);
   430	        }
   431	
   432	        let (received_bytes, control_messages) =
   433	            self.block_on(IoEvents::IN, || self.try_recv(writer, flags))?;
   434	
   435	        let message_header = MessageHeader::new(None, control_messages);
   436	
   437	        Ok((received_bytes, message_header))
   438	    }
   439	
   440	    fn get_option(&self, option: &mut dyn SocketOption) -> Result<()> {
   441	        let state = self.state.read();
   442	        let options = self.options.read();
   443	
   444	        // Deal with UNIX-socket-specific socket-level options
   445	        match do_unix_getsockopt(option, state.as_ref()) {
   446	            Err(err) if err.error() == Errno::ENOPROTOOPT => (),
   447	            res => return res,
   448	        }
   449	
   450	        // Deal with socket-level options
   451	        match options.socket.get_option(option, state.as_ref()) {
   452	            Err(err) if err.error() == Errno::ENOPROTOOPT => (),
   453	            res => return res,
   454	        }
   455	
   456	        // TODO: Deal with socket options from other levels
   457	        warn!("only socket-level options are supported");
   458	
   459	        return_errno_with_message!(Errno::ENOPROTOOPT, "the socket option to get is unknown")
   460	    }
   461	
   462	    fn set_option(&self, option: &dyn SocketOption) -> Result<()> {
   463	        let mut state = self.state.write();
   464	        let mut options = self.options.write();
   465	
   466	        match options.socket.set_option(option, state.as_mut()) {
   467	            Ok(_) => Ok(()),
   468	            Err(err) if err.error() == Errno::ENOPROTOOPT => {
   469	                // TODO: Deal with socket options from other levels
   470	                warn!("only socket-level options are supported");
     1	// SPDX-License-Identifier: MPL-2.0

     3	use align_ext::AlignExt;
     4	
     5	use super::SocketAddr;
     6	use crate::{net::socket::unix::UnixControlMessage, prelude::*, util::net::CSocketOptionLevel};

     8	/// Message header used for sendmsg/recvmsg.
     9	#[derive(Debug)]
    10	pub struct MessageHeader {
    11	    pub(in crate::net) addr: Option<SocketAddr>,
    12	    pub(in crate::net) control_messages: Vec<ControlMessage>,
    13	}
    14	
    15	impl MessageHeader {
    16	    /// Creates a new `MessageHeader`.
    17	    pub const fn new(addr: Option<SocketAddr>, control_messages: Vec<ControlMessage>) -> Self {
    18	        Self {
    19	            addr,
    20	            control_messages,
    21	        }
    22	    }
    23	
    24	    /// Returns the socket address.
    25	    pub fn addr(&self) -> Option<&SocketAddr> {
    26	        self.addr.as_ref()
    27	    }
    28	
    29	    /// Returns the control messages.
    30	    pub fn control_messages(&self) -> &Vec<ControlMessage> {
    31	        &self.control_messages
    32	    }
    33	}
    34	
    35	/// Control messages in [`MessageHeader`].
    36	#[derive(Debug)]
    37	pub enum ControlMessage {
    38	    Unix(UnixControlMessage),
    39	}
    40	
    41	impl ControlMessage {
    42	    pub fn read_all_from(reader: &mut VmReader) -> Result<Vec<Self>> {
    43	        // FIXME: This method may exhaust kernel memory and cause a panic if the program is
    44	        // malicious and attempts to send too many control messages. To prevent this, we limit the
    45	        // number of control messages, but this limit does not have a Linux equivalent.
    46	        const MAX_NR_MSGS: usize = 32;
    47	
    48	        let mut msgs = Vec::new();
    49	
    50	        while reader.has_remain() && msgs.len() < MAX_NR_MSGS {
    51	            let header = reader.read_val::<CControlHeader>()?;
    52	            if header.len <= size_of::<CControlHeader>() || header.payload_len() > reader.remain() {
    53	                return_errno_with_message!(
    54	                    Errno::EINVAL,
    55	                    "the size of the control message is invalid"
    56	                );
    57	            }
    58	
    59	            if let Some(msg) = Self::read_from(&header, reader)? {
    60	                msgs.push(msg);
    61	            }
    62	
    63	            let padding_len = header.padding_len().min(reader.remain());
    64	            reader.skip(padding_len);
    65	        }
    66	
    67	        if reader.has_remain() {
    68	            warn!("excessive control messages are currently not permitted");
    69	            return_errno_with_message!(
    70	                Errno::E2BIG,
    71	                "excessive control messages are currently not permitted"
    72	            );
    73	        }
    74	
    75	        Ok(msgs)
    76	    }
    77	
    78	    fn read_from(header: &CControlHeader, reader: &mut VmReader) -> Result<Option<Self>> {
    79	        let Some(level) = header.level() else {
    80	            warn!("unsupported control message level in {:?}", header);
    81	            reader.skip(header.payload_len());
    82	            return Ok(None);
    83	        };
    84	
    85	        match level {
    86	            CSocketOptionLevel::SOL_SOCKET => {
    87	                // Linux manual pages say (https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man7/unix.7.html):
    88	                // "For historical reasons, the ancillary message types listed below are specified
    89	                // with a SOL_SOCKET type even though they are AF_UNIX specific."
    90	                let msg = UnixControlMessage::read_from(header, reader)?;
    91	                Ok(msg.map(Self::Unix))
    92	            }
    93	            _ => {
    94	                warn!("unsupported control message level in {:?}", header);
    95	                reader.skip(header.payload_len());
    96	                Ok(None)
    97	            }
    98	        }
    99	    }
   100	
   101	    pub fn write_all_to(msgs: &[Self], writer: &mut VmWriter) -> usize {
   102	        let mut len = 0;
   103	
   104	        for msg in msgs.iter() {
   105	            let header = match msg.write_to(writer) {
   106	                Ok(header) => header,
   107	                // This occurs when the buffer is too short or when some page faults cannot be
   108	                // handled. However, at this point, there is no good way to report the errors to
   109	                // user space. According to the Linux implementation, it seems okay to silently
   110	                // ignore errors here.
   111	                Err(_) => {
   112	                    warn!("setting MSG_CTRUNC is not supported");
   113	                    break;
   114	                }
   115	            };
   116	
   117	            len += header.total_len();
   118	
   119	            let padding_len = header.padding_len().min(writer.avail());
   120	            writer.skip(padding_len);
   121	            len += padding_len;
   122	        }
   123	
   124	        len
   125	    }
   126	
   127	    fn write_to(&self, writer: &mut VmWriter) -> Result<CControlHeader> {
   128	        match self {
   129	            Self::Unix(msg) => msg.write_to(writer),
   130	        }
   131	    }
   132	}
   133	
   134	/// `cmsghdr` in Linux.
   135	///
   136	/// Reference: <https://elixir.bootlin.com/linux/v6.13/source/include/linux/socket.h#L105>.
   137	#[repr(C)]
   138	#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, Pod)]
   139	pub struct CControlHeader {
   140	    /// Data byte count, including hdr
   141	    len: usize,
   142	    /// Originating protocol
   143	    level: i32,
   144	    /// Protocol-specific type
   145	    type_: i32,
   146	}
   147	
   148	/// Alignment of control messages.
   149	///
   150	/// Reference: <https://elixir.bootlin.com/linux/v6.13/source/include/linux/socket.h#L119>.
   151	const CMSG_ALIGN: usize = size_of::<usize>();
   152	
   153	impl CControlHeader {
   154	    /// Creates a control message header with the level, type, and payload length.
   155	    pub fn new(level: CSocketOptionLevel, type_: i32, payload_len: usize) -> Self {
   156	        Self {
   157	            len: payload_len + size_of::<Self>(),
   158	            level: level as i32,
   159	            type_,
   160	        }
   161	    }
   162	
   163	    /// Computes the payload length from the total length.
   164	    pub fn payload_len_from_total(total_len: usize) -> Result<usize> {
   165	        total_len.checked_sub(size_of::<Self>()).ok_or_else(|| {
   166	            Error::with_message(Errno::EINVAL, "the control message buffer is too small")
   167	        })
   168	    }
   169	
   170	    /// Returns the level of the control message.
   171	    pub fn level(&self) -> Option<CSocketOptionLevel> {
   172	        CSocketOptionLevel::try_from(self.level).ok()
   173	    }
   174	
   175	    /// Returns the type of the control message.
   176	    pub fn type_(&self) -> i32 {
   177	        self.type_
   178	    }
   179	
   180	    /// Returns the payload length of the control message.
   181	    pub fn payload_len(&self) -> usize {
   182	        self.len - size_of::<Self>()
   183	    }
   184	
   185	    /// Returns the length of the control message (payload + header, excluding paddings).
   186	    pub fn total_len(&self) -> usize {
   187	        self.len
   188	    }
   189	
   190	    /// Returns the length of the padding bytes for the control message.
   191	    pub(self) fn padding_len(&self) -> usize {
   192	        self.total_len_with_padding() - self.total_len()
   193	    }
   194	
   195	    /// Returns the length of the control message (payload + header, including paddings).
   196	    fn total_len_with_padding(&self) -> usize {
   197	        self.len.align_up(CMSG_ALIGN)
   198	    }
   199	}

     3	use core::{
     4	    marker::PhantomData,
     5	    num::Wrapping,
     6	    ops::Deref,
     7	    sync::atomic::{AtomicUsize, Ordering},
     8	};
     9	
    10	use inherit_methods_macro::inherit_methods;
    11	use ostd::mm::{FrameAllocOptions, Segment, UntypedMem, VmIo};
    12	
    13	use super::{MultiRead, MultiWrite};
    14	use crate::prelude::*;
    15	
    16	/// A lock-free SPSC FIFO ring buffer backed by a [`Segment<()>`].
    17	///
    18	/// The ring buffer supports `push`/`pop` any `T: Pod` items, also
    19	/// supports `write`/`read` any bytes data based on [`VmReader`]/[`VmWriter`].
    20	///
    21	/// The ring buffer returns immediately after processing without any blocking.
    22	/// The ring buffer can be shared between threads.
    23	///
    24	/// # Example
    25	///
    26	/// ```
    27	/// use ostd_pod::Pod;
    28	/// use ring_buffer::RingBuffer;
    29	///
    30	/// #[derive(Pod)]
    31	/// struct Item {
    32	///     a: u32,
    33	///     b: u32,
    34	/// }
    35	///
    36	/// let rb = RingBuffer::<Item>::new(10);
    37	/// let (producer, consumer) = rb.split();
    38	///
    39	/// for i in 0..10 {
    40	///     producer.push(Item { a: i, b: i }).unwrap();
    41	/// }
    42	///
    43	/// for _ in 0..10 {
    44	///     let item = consumer.pop().unwrap();
    45	///     assert_eq!(item.a, item.b);
    46	/// }
    47	/// ```
    48	pub struct RingBuffer<T> {
    49	    segment: Segment<()>,
    50	    capacity: usize,
    51	    tail: AtomicUsize,
    52	    head: AtomicUsize,
    53	    phantom: PhantomData<T>,
    54	}
    55	
    56	/// A producer of a [`RingBuffer`].
    57	pub struct Producer<T, R: Deref<Target = RingBuffer<T>>> {
    58	    rb: R,
    59	    phantom: PhantomData<T>,
    60	}
    61	/// A consumer of a [`RingBuffer`].
    62	pub struct Consumer<T, R: Deref<Target = RingBuffer<T>>> {
    63	    rb: R,
    64	    phantom: PhantomData<T>,
    65	}
    66	
    67	pub type RbProducer<T> = Producer<T, Arc<RingBuffer<T>>>;
    68	pub type RbConsumer<T> = Consumer<T, Arc<RingBuffer<T>>>;
    69	
    70	impl<T> RingBuffer<T> {
    71	    const T_SIZE: usize = core::mem::size_of::<T>();
    72	
    73	    /// Creates a new [`RingBuffer`] with the given capacity.
    74	    pub fn new(capacity: usize) -> Self {
    75	        assert!(
    76	            capacity.is_power_of_two(),
    77	            "capacity must be a power of two"
    78	        );
    79	
    80	        let nframes = capacity
    81	            .checked_mul(Self::T_SIZE)
    82	            .unwrap()
    83	            .div_ceil(PAGE_SIZE);
    84	        let segment = FrameAllocOptions::new()
    85	            .zeroed(false)
    86	            .alloc_segment(nframes)
    87	            .unwrap();
    88	
    89	        Self {
    90	            segment,
    91	            capacity,
    92	            tail: AtomicUsize::new(0),
    93	            head: AtomicUsize::new(0),
    94	            phantom: PhantomData,
    95	        }
    96	    }
    97	
    98	    /// Splits the [`RingBuffer`] into a producer and a consumer.
    99	    pub fn split(self) -> (RbProducer<T>, RbConsumer<T>) {
   100	        let producer = Producer {
   101	            rb: Arc::new(self),
   102	            phantom: PhantomData,
   103	        };
   104	        let consumer = Consumer {
   105	            rb: Arc::clone(&producer.rb),
   106	            phantom: PhantomData,
   107	        };
   108	        (producer, consumer)
   109	    }
   110	
   111	    /// Gets the capacity of the `RingBuffer`.
   112	    pub fn capacity(&self) -> usize {
   113	        self.capacity
   114	    }
   115	
   116	    /// Checks if the `RingBuffer` is empty.
   117	    pub fn is_empty(&self) -> bool {
   118	        self.len() == 0
   119	    }
   120	
   121	    /// Checks if the `RingBuffer` is full.
   122	    pub fn is_full(&self) -> bool {
   123	        self.free_len() == 0
   124	    }
   125	
   126	    /// Gets the number of items in the `RingBuffer`.
   127	    pub fn len(&self) -> usize {
   128	        // Implementation notes: This subtraction only makes sense if either the head or the tail
   129	        // is considered frozen; if both are volatile, the number of the items may become negative
   130	        // due to race conditions. This is always true with a `RingBuffer` or a pair of
   131	        // `RbProducer` and `RbConsumer`.
   132	        (self.tail() - self.head()).0
   133	    }
   134	
   135	    /// Gets the number of free items in the `RingBuffer`.
   136	    pub fn free_len(&self) -> usize {
   137	        self.capacity - self.len()
   138	    }
   139	
   140	    /// Gets the head number of the `RingBuffer`.
   141	    ///
   142	    /// This is the number of items read from the ring buffer. The number wraps when crossing
   143	    /// [`usize`] boundaries.
   144	    pub fn head(&self) -> Wrapping<usize> {
   145	        Wrapping(self.head.load(Ordering::Acquire))
   146	    }
   147	
   148	    /// Gets the tail number of the `RingBuffer`.
   149	    ///
   150	    /// This is the number of items written into the ring buffer. The number wraps when crossing
   151	    /// [`usize`] boundaries.
   152	    pub fn tail(&self) -> Wrapping<usize> {
   153	        Wrapping(self.tail.load(Ordering::Acquire))
   154	    }
   155	
   156	    /// Clears the `RingBuffer`.
   157	    pub fn clear(&mut self) {
   158	        self.tail.store(0, Ordering::Release);
   159	        self.head.store(0, Ordering::Release);
   160	    }
   161	}
   162	
   163	impl<T: Pod> RingBuffer<T> {
   164	    /// Pushes an item to the `RingBuffer`.
   165	    ///
   166	    /// Returns `Some` on success. Returns `None` if
   167	    /// the ring buffer is full.
   168	    pub fn push(&mut self, item: T) -> Option<()> {
   169	        let mut producer = Producer {
   170	            rb: self,
   171	            phantom: PhantomData,
   172	        };
   173	        producer.push(item)
   174	    }
   175	
   176	    /// Pushes a slice of items to the `RingBuffer`.
   177	    ///
   178	    /// Returns `Some` on success, all items are pushed to the ring buffer.
   179	    /// Returns `None` if the ring buffer is full or cannot fit all items.
   180	    pub fn push_slice(&mut self, items: &[T]) -> Option<()> {
   181	        let mut producer = Producer {
   182	            rb: self,
   183	            phantom: PhantomData,
   184	        };
   185	        producer.push_slice(items)
   186	    }
   187	
   188	    /// Pops an item from the `RingBuffer`.
   189	    ///
   190	    /// Returns `Some` with the popped item on success.
   191	    /// Returns `None` if the ring buffer is empty.
   192	    pub fn pop(&mut self) -> Option<T> {
   193	        let mut consumer = Consumer {
   194	            rb: self,
   195	            phantom: PhantomData,
   196	        };
   197	        consumer.pop()
   198	    }
   199	
   200	    /// Pops a slice of items from the `RingBuffer`.
   201	    ///
   202	    /// Returns `Some` on success, all items are popped from the ring buffer.
   203	    /// Returns `None` if the ring buffer is empty or cannot fill all items.
   204	    pub fn pop_slice(&mut self, items: &mut [T]) -> Option<()> {
   205	        let mut consumer = Consumer {
   206	            rb: self,
   207	            phantom: PhantomData,
   208	        };
   209	        consumer.pop_slice(items)
   210	    }
   211	
   212	    pub(self) fn advance_tail(&self, mut tail: Wrapping<usize>, len: usize) {
   213	        tail += len;
   214	        self.tail.store(tail.0, Ordering::Release);
   215	    }
   216	
   217	    pub(self) fn advance_head(&self, mut head: Wrapping<usize>, len: usize) {
   218	        head += len;
   219	        self.head.store(head.0, Ordering::Release);
   220	    }
   221	}
   222	
   223	impl RingBuffer<u8> {
   224	    /// Writes data from the `reader` to the `RingBuffer`.
   225	    ///
   226	    /// Returns the number of bytes written.
   227	    #[expect(unused)]
   228	    pub fn write_fallible(&mut self, reader: &mut dyn MultiRead) -> Result<usize> {
   229	        let mut producer = Producer {
   230	            rb: self,
   231	            phantom: PhantomData,
   232	        };
   233	        producer.write_fallible(reader)
   234	    }
   235	
   236	    /// Reads data from the `writer` to the `RingBuffer`.
   237	    ///
   238	    /// Returns the number of bytes read.
   239	    pub fn read_fallible(&mut self, writer: &mut dyn MultiWrite) -> Result<usize> {
   240	        let mut consumer = Consumer {
   241	            rb: self,
   242	            phantom: PhantomData,
   243	        };
   244	        consumer.read_fallible(writer)
   245	    }
   246	}
   247	
   248	impl<T: Pod, R: Deref<Target = RingBuffer<T>>> Producer<T, R> {
   249	    const T_SIZE: usize = core::mem::size_of::<T>();
   250	
   251	    /// Pushes an item to the `RingBuffer`.
   252	    ///
   253	    /// Returns `Some` on success. Returns `None` if
   254	    /// the ring buffer is full.
   255	    pub fn push(&mut self, item: T) -> Option<()> {
   256	        let rb = &self.rb;
   257	        if rb.is_full() {
   258	            return None;
   259	        }
   260	
   261	        let tail = rb.tail();
   262	        let offset = tail.0 & (rb.capacity - 1);
   263	        let byte_offset = offset * Self::T_SIZE;
   264	
   265	        let mut writer = rb.segment.writer();
   266	        writer.skip(byte_offset);
   267	        writer.write_val(&item).unwrap();
   268	
   269	        rb.advance_tail(tail, 1);
   270	        Some(())
   271	    }
   272	
   273	    /// Pushes a slice of items to the `RingBuffer`.
   274	    ///
   275	    /// Returns `Some` on success, all items are pushed to the ring buffer.
   276	    /// Returns `None` if the ring buffer is full or cannot fit all items.
   277	    pub fn push_slice(&mut self, items: &[T]) -> Option<()> {
   278	        let rb = &self.rb;
   279	        let nitems = items.len();
   280	        if rb.free_len() < nitems {
   281	            return None;
   282	        }
   283	
   284	        let tail = rb.tail();
   285	        let offset = tail.0 & (rb.capacity - 1);
   286	        let byte_offset = offset * Self::T_SIZE;
   287	
   288	        if offset + nitems > rb.capacity {
   289	            // Write into two separate parts
   290	            rb.segment
   291	                .write_slice(byte_offset, &items[..rb.capacity - offset])
   292	                .unwrap();
   293	            rb.segment
   294	                .write_slice(0, &items[rb.capacity - offset..])
   295	                .unwrap();
   296	        } else {
   297	            rb.segment.write_slice(byte_offset, items).unwrap();
   298	        }
   299	
   300	        rb.advance_tail(tail, nitems);
   300	        rb.advance_tail(tail, nitems);
   301	        Some(())
   302	    }
   303	}
   304	
   305	impl<R: Deref<Target = RingBuffer<u8>>> Producer<u8, R> {
   306	    /// Writes data from the `VmReader` to the `RingBuffer`.
   307	    ///
   308	    /// Returns the number of bytes written.
   309	    pub fn write_fallible(&mut self, reader: &mut dyn MultiRead) -> Result<usize> {
   310	        self.write_fallible_with_max_len(reader, usize::MAX)
   311	    }
   312	
   313	    /// Writes data from the `VmReader` to the `RingBuffer` with the maximum length.
   314	    ///
   315	    /// Returns the number of bytes written.
   316	    pub fn write_fallible_with_max_len(
   317	        &mut self,
   318	        reader: &mut dyn MultiRead,
   319	        max_len: usize,
   320	    ) -> Result<usize> {
   321	        let rb = &self.rb;
   322	        let free_len = rb.free_len().min(max_len);
   323	
   324	        let tail = rb.tail();
   325	        let offset = tail.0 & (rb.capacity - 1);
   326	
   327	        let write_len = if offset + free_len > rb.capacity {
   328	            // Write into two separate parts
   329	            let mut write_len = 0;
   330	
   331	            let mut writer = rb.segment.writer();
   332	            writer.skip(offset).limit(rb.capacity - offset);
   333	            write_len += reader.read(&mut writer)?;
   334	
   335	            let mut writer = rb.segment.writer();
   336	            writer.limit(free_len - (rb.capacity - offset));
   337	            write_len += reader.read(&mut writer)?;
   338	
   339	            write_len
   340	        } else {
   341	            let mut writer = rb.segment.writer();
   342	            writer.skip(offset).limit(free_len);
   343	            reader.read(&mut writer)?
   344	        };
   345	
   346	        rb.advance_tail(tail, write_len);
   347	        Ok(write_len)
   348	    }
   349	
   350	    // There is no counterpart to `Consumer::skip`. It does not make sense for the producer.
   351	}
   352	
   353	#[inherit_methods(from = "self.rb")]
   354	impl<T, R: Deref<Target = RingBuffer<T>>> Producer<T, R> {
   355	    pub fn capacity(&self) -> usize;
   356	    pub fn is_empty(&self) -> bool;
   357	    pub fn is_full(&self) -> bool;
   358	    pub fn len(&self) -> usize;
   359	    pub fn free_len(&self) -> usize;
   360	    pub fn head(&self) -> Wrapping<usize>;
   361	    pub fn tail(&self) -> Wrapping<usize>;
   362	}
   363	
   364	impl<T: Pod, R: Deref<Target = RingBuffer<T>>> Consumer<T, R> {
   365	    const T_SIZE: usize = core::mem::size_of::<T>();
   366	
   367	    /// Pops an item from the `RingBuffer`.
   368	    ///
   369	    /// Returns `Some` with the popped item on success.
   370	    /// Returns `None` if the ring buffer is empty.
   371	    pub fn pop(&mut self) -> Option<T> {
   372	        let rb = &self.rb;
   373	        if rb.is_empty() {
   374	            return None;
   375	        }
   376	
   377	        let head = rb.head();
   378	        let offset = head.0 & (rb.capacity - 1);
   379	        let byte_offset = offset * Self::T_SIZE;
   380	
   381	        let mut reader = rb.segment.reader();
   382	        reader.skip(byte_offset);
   383	        let item = reader.read_val::<T>().unwrap();
   384	
   385	        rb.advance_head(head, 1);
   386	        Some(item)
   387	    }
   388	
   389	    /// Pops a slice of items from the `RingBuffer`.
   390	    ///
   391	    /// Returns `Some` on success, all items are popped from the ring buffer.
   392	    /// Returns `None` if the ring buffer is empty or cannot fill all items.
   393	    pub fn pop_slice(&mut self, items: &mut [T]) -> Option<()> {
   394	        let rb = &self.rb;
   395	        let nitems = items.len();
   396	        if rb.len() < nitems {
   397	            return None;
   398	        }
   399	
   400	        let head = rb.head();
   401	        let offset = head.0 & (rb.capacity - 1);
   402	        let byte_offset = offset * Self::T_SIZE;
   403	
   404	        if offset + nitems > rb.capacity {
   405	            // Read from two separate parts
   406	            rb.segment
   407	                .read_slice(byte_offset, &mut items[..rb.capacity - offset])
   408	                .unwrap();
   409	            rb.segment
   410	                .read_slice(0, &mut items[rb.capacity - offset..])
   411	                .unwrap();
   412	        } else {
   413	            rb.segment.read_slice(byte_offset, items).unwrap();
   414	        }
   415	
   416	        rb.advance_head(head, nitems);
   417	        Some(())
   418	    }
   419	}
   420	
   421	impl<R: Deref<Target = RingBuffer<u8>>> Consumer<u8, R> {
   422	    /// Reads data from the `VmWriter` to the `RingBuffer`.
   423	    ///
   424	    /// Returns the number of bytes read.
   425	    pub fn read_fallible(&mut self, writer: &mut dyn MultiWrite) -> Result<usize> {
   426	        self.read_fallible_with_max_len(writer, usize::MAX)
   427	    }
   428	
   429	    /// Reads data from the `VmWriter` to the `RingBuffer` with the maximum length.
   430	    ///
   431	    /// Returns the number of bytes read.
   432	    pub fn read_fallible_with_max_len(
   433	        &mut self,
   434	        writer: &mut dyn MultiWrite,
   435	        max_len: usize,
   436	    ) -> Result<usize> {
   437	        let rb = &self.rb;
   438	        let len = rb.len().min(max_len);
   439	
   440	        let head = rb.head();
   441	        let offset = head.0 & (rb.capacity - 1);
   442	
   443	        let read_len = if offset + len > rb.capacity {
   444	            // Read from two separate parts
   445	            let mut read_len = 0;
   446	
   447	            let mut reader = rb.segment.reader();
   448	            reader.skip(offset).limit(rb.capacity - offset);
   449	            read_len += writer.write(&mut reader)?;
   450	
   451	            let mut reader = rb.segment.reader();
   452	            reader.limit(len - (rb.capacity - offset));
   453	            read_len += writer.write(&mut reader)?;
   454	
   455	            read_len
   456	        } else {
   457	            let mut reader = rb.segment.reader();
   458	            reader.skip(offset).limit(len);
   459	            writer.write(&mut reader)?
   460	        };
   461	
   462	        rb.advance_head(head, read_len);
   463	        Ok(read_len)
   464	    }
   465	
   466	    /// Skips `count` bytes in the `RingBuffer`.
   467	    ///
   468	    /// In other words, `count` bytes are read from the `RingBuffer` and discarded.
   469	    ///
   470	    /// # Panics
   471	    ///
   472	    /// This method will panic if the number of the available bytes to read is less than `count`.
   473	    pub fn skip(&mut self, count: usize) {
   474	        let rb = &self.rb;
   475	        let len = rb.len();
   476	        assert!(len >= count);
   477	
   478	        let head = rb.head();
   479	        rb.advance_head(head, count);
   480	    }
   481	}
   482	
   483	#[inherit_methods(from = "self.rb")]
   484	impl<T, R: Deref<Target = RingBuffer<T>>> Consumer<T, R> {
   485	    pub fn capacity(&self) -> usize;
   486	    pub fn is_empty(&self) -> bool;
   487	    pub fn is_full(&self) -> bool;
   488	    pub fn len(&self) -> usize;
   489	    pub fn free_len(&self) -> usize;
   490	    pub fn head(&self) -> Wrapping<usize>;
   491	    pub fn tail(&self) -> Wrapping<usize>;
   492	}

   495	mod test {
   496	    use ostd::prelude::*;
   497	
   498	    use super::*;
   499	
   500	    #[ktest]
   501	    fn test_rb_basics() {
   502	        let mut rb = RingBuffer::<i32>::new(4);
   503	        rb.push(-100).unwrap();
   504	        rb.push_slice(&[-1]).unwrap();
   505	        assert_eq!(rb.len(), 2);
   506	
   507	        let mut popped = [0i32; 2];
   508	        rb.pop_slice(&mut popped).unwrap();
   509	        assert_eq!(popped, [-100i32, -1]);
   510	        assert!(rb.is_empty());
   511	
   512	        rb.push_slice(&[i32::MAX, 1, -2, 100]).unwrap();
   513	        assert!(rb.is_full());
   514	
   515	        let popped = rb.pop();
   516	        assert_eq!(popped, Some(i32::MAX));
   517	        assert_eq!(rb.free_len(), 1);
   518	
   519	        let mut popped = [0i32; 3];
   520	        rb.pop_slice(&mut popped).unwrap();
   521	        assert_eq!(popped, [1i32, -2, 100]);
   522	        assert_eq!(rb.free_len(), 4);
   523	    }
   524	
   525	    #[ktest]
   526	    fn test_rb_write_read_one() {
   527	        let rb = RingBuffer::<u8>::new(1);
   528	
   529	        let (mut prod, mut cons) = rb.split();
   530	        assert_eq!(prod.capacity(), 1);
   531	        assert_eq!(cons.capacity(), 1);
   532	
   533	        assert!(cons.pop().is_none());
   534	        assert!(prod.push(1).is_some());
   535	        assert!(prod.is_full());
   536	
   537	        assert!(prod.push(2).is_none());
   538	        assert!(prod.push_slice(&[2]).is_none());
   539	        assert_eq!(cons.pop().unwrap(), 1u8);
   540	        assert!(cons.is_empty());
   541	
   542	        let input = [u8::MAX];
   543	        assert_eq!(
   544	            prod.write_fallible(&mut reader_from(input.as_slice()))
   545	                .unwrap(),
   546	            1
   547	        );
   548	        assert_eq!(
   549	            prod.write_fallible(&mut reader_from(input.as_slice()))
   550	                .unwrap(),
   551	            0
   552	        );
   553	        assert_eq!(prod.len(), 1);
   554	
   555	        let mut output = [0u8];
   556	        assert_eq!(
   557	            cons.read_fallible(&mut writer_from(output.as_mut_slice()))
   558	                .unwrap(),
   559	            1
   560	        );
   561	        assert_eq!(
   562	            cons.read_fallible(&mut writer_from(output.as_mut_slice()))
   563	                .unwrap(),
   564	            0
   565	        );
   566	        assert_eq!(cons.free_len(), 1);
   567	
   568	        assert_eq!(output, input);
   569	    }
   570	
   571	    #[ktest]
   572	    fn test_rb_write_read_all() {
   573	        let rb = RingBuffer::<u8>::new(4 * PAGE_SIZE);
   574	        assert_eq!(rb.capacity(), 4 * PAGE_SIZE);
   575	
   576	        let (mut prod, mut cons) = rb.split();
   577	        prod.push(u8::MIN).unwrap();
   578	        assert_eq!(cons.pop().unwrap(), u8::MIN);
   579	
   580	        prod.push_slice(&[u8::MAX]).unwrap();
   581	        let mut popped = [0u8];
   582	        cons.pop_slice(&mut popped).unwrap();
   583	        assert_eq!(popped, [u8::MAX]);
   584	
   585	        let step = 128;
   586	        let mut input = vec![0u8; step];
   587	        for i in (0..4 * PAGE_SIZE).step_by(step) {
   588	            input.fill(i as _);
   589	            let write_len = prod
   590	                .write_fallible(&mut reader_from(input.as_slice()))
   591	                .unwrap();
   592	            assert_eq!(write_len, step);
   593	        }
   594	        assert!(cons.is_full());
   595	
   596	        let mut output = vec![0u8; step];
   597	        for i in (0..4 * PAGE_SIZE).step_by(step) {
   598	            let read_len = cons
   599	                .read_fallible(&mut writer_from(output.as_mut_slice()))
   600	                .unwrap();
   601	            assert_eq!(read_len, step);
   602	            assert_eq!(output[0], i as u8);
   603	            assert_eq!(output[step - 1], i as u8);
   604	        }
   605	        assert!(prod.is_empty());
   606	    }
   607	
   608	    fn reader_from(buf: &[u8]) -> VmReader {
   609	        VmReader::from(buf).to_fallible()
   610	    }
   611	
   612	    fn writer_from(buf: &mut [u8]) -> VmWriter {
   613	        VmWriter::from(buf).to_fallible()
   614	    }
   615	}

    35		memset(&mhdr, 0, sizeof(mhdr));

    51		cdata[0] = cfds[0];
    52		cdata[1] = cfds[0];
    53		cdata[2] = cfds[1];
    54	
    55		// Sending control messages with zero bytes to a stream socket
    56		// seems to "succeed". However, no data or control messages can
    57		// be transmitted.
    58		mhdr.msg_iovlen = 0;
    59		TEST_SUCC(sendmsg(fildes[0], &mhdr, 0));
    60		mhdr.msg_iovlen = 1;
    61	
    62		// > (1)  sendmsg(2) of four bytes, with no ancillary data.
    63		// > (2)  sendmsg(2) of one byte, with ancillary data.
    64		// > (3)  sendmsg(2) of four bytes, with no ancillary data.
    65		//  -- https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man7/unix.7.html
    66		TEST_RES(send(fildes[0], buf, 4, 0), _ret == 4);
    67		TEST_RES(sendmsg(fildes[0], &mhdr, 0), _ret == 1);
    68		TEST_RES(send(fildes[0], buf, 4, 0), _ret == 4);
    69	
    70		memset(&mhdr, 0, sizeof(mhdr));
    71		mhdr.msg_iov = &iov;
    72		mhdr.msg_iovlen = 1;
    73		mhdr.msg_control = cbuf;
    74		mhdr.msg_controllen = CMSG_SPACE(sizeof(int));
    75	
    76		iov.iov_base = buf;
    77		iov.iov_len = sizeof(buf);
    78	
    79		memset(cbuf, 0, sizeof(cbuf));

    81		// > Suppose that the receiver now performs recvmsg(2) calls each with
    82		// > a buffer size of 20 bytes.  The first call will receive five bytes
    83		// > of data, along with the ancillary data sent by the second
    84		// > sendmsg(2) call.
    85		TEST_RES(recvmsg(fildes[1], &mhdr, 0),
    86			 _ret == 5 &&
    87				 mhdr.msg_controllen == CMSG_SPACE(sizeof(int) * 2) &&

    92				 (cdata = (int *)CMSG_DATA(chdr)) &&
    93				 cdata[0] == cfds[1] + 1 && cdata[1] == cfds[1] + 2);
    94		// > The next call will receive the remaining four
    95		// > bytes of data.
    96		TEST_RES(recv(fildes[1], buf, sizeof(buf), 0), _ret == 4);

    15	    prelude::*,
    16	    process::{credentials::capabilities::CapSet, posix_thread::AsPosixThread},
    17	    util::net::CSocketOptionLevel,
    18	};
    19	
    20	#[derive(Debug)]
    21	pub struct UnixControlMessage(Message);
    22	
    23	#[derive(Debug)]
    24	enum Message {
    25	    Files(FileMessage),
    26	    Cred(CredMessage),
    27	}
    28	
    29	impl UnixControlMessage {
    30	    pub fn read_from(header: &CControlHeader, reader: &mut VmReader) -> Result<Option<Self>> {
    31	        debug_assert_eq!(header.level(), Some(CSocketOptionLevel::SOL_SOCKET));
    32	
    33	        let Ok(type_) = CControlType::try_from(header.type_()) else {
    34	            warn!("unsupported control message type in {:?}", header);
    35	            reader.skip(header.payload_len());
    36	            return Ok(None);
    37	        };
    38	
    39	        match type_ {
    40	            CControlType::SCM_RIGHTS => {
    41	                let msg = FileMessage::read_from(header, reader)?;
    42	                Ok(Some(Self(Message::Files(msg))))
    43	            }
    44	            CControlType::SCM_CREDENTIALS => {
    45	                let msg = CredMessage::read_from(header, reader)?;
    46	                Ok(Some(Self(Message::Cred(msg))))
    47	            }
    48	            _ => {
    49	                warn!("unsupported control message type in {:?}", header);
    50	                reader.skip(header.payload_len());
    51	                Ok(None)
    52	            }
    53	        }
    54	    }
    55	
    56	    pub fn write_to(&self, writer: &mut VmWriter) -> Result<CControlHeader> {
    57	        match &self.0 {
    58	            Message::Files(msg) => msg.write_to(writer),
    59	            Message::Cred(msg) => msg.write_to(writer),
    60	        }
    61	    }
    62	}
    63	
    64	struct FileMessage {
    65	    files: Vec<Arc<dyn FileLike>>,
    66	}
    67	
    68	impl fmt::Debug for FileMessage {
    69	    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
    70	        f.debug_struct("FileMessage")
    71	            .field("len", &self.files.len())
    72	            .finish_non_exhaustive()
    73	    }
    74	}
    75	
    76	/// The maximum number of the file descriptors in the control messages.
    77	///
    78	/// Reference: <https://elixir.bootlin.com/linux/v6.15/source/include/net/scm.h#L18>.
    79	const MAX_NR_FILES: usize = 253;
    80	
    81	impl FileMessage {
    82	    fn read_from(header: &CControlHeader, reader: &mut VmReader) -> Result<Self> {
    83	        let payload_len = header.payload_len();
    84	        if payload_len % size_of::<i32>() != 0 {
    85	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EINVAL, "the SCM_RIGHTS message is invalid");
    86	        }
    87	        let nfiles = payload_len / size_of::<i32>();
    88	
    89	        // "Attempting to send an array larger than this limit causes sendmsg(2) to fail with the
    90	        // error EINVAL." -- Reference: <https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man7/unix.7.html>.
    91	        if nfiles > MAX_NR_FILES {
    92	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EINVAL, "the SCM_RIGHTS message is too large");
    93	        }
    94	        // TODO: "[the ETOOMANYREFS error] occurs if the number of "in-flight" file descriptors
    95	        // exceeds the RLIMIT_NOFILE resource limit and the caller does not have the
    96	        // CAP_SYS_RESOURCE capability."
    97	
    98	        let mut files = Vec::with_capacity(nfiles);
    99	
   100	        let current = Task::current().unwrap();
   101	        let mut file_table = current.as_thread_local().unwrap().borrow_file_table_mut();
   102	        for _ in 0..nfiles {
   103	            let fd = reader.read_val::<i32>()?;
   104	            let file = get_file_fast!(&mut file_table, fd).into_owned();
   105	            files.push(file);
   106	        }
   107	
   108	        Ok(FileMessage { files })
   109	    }
   110	
   111	    fn write_to(&self, writer: &mut VmWriter) -> Result<CControlHeader> {
   112	        let nfiles = self
   113	            .files
   114	            .len()
   115	            .min(CControlHeader::payload_len_from_total(writer.avail())? / size_of::<i32>());
   116	        if nfiles == 0 {
   117	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EINVAL, "the control message buffer is too small");
   118	        }
   119	        if nfiles < self.files.len() {
   120	            warn!("setting MSG_CTRUNC is not supported");
   121	        }
   122	
   123	        let header = CControlHeader::new(
   124	            CSocketOptionLevel::SOL_SOCKET,
   125	            CControlType::SCM_RIGHTS as i32,
   126	            nfiles * size_of::<i32>(),
   127	        );
   128	        writer.write_val::<CControlHeader>(&header)?;
   129	
   130	        let current = Task::current().unwrap();
   131	        let file_table = current.as_thread_local().unwrap().borrow_file_table();
   132	        for file in self.files[..nfiles].iter() {
   133	            // TODO: Deal with the `O_CLOEXEC` flag.
   134	            let fd = file_table
   135	                .unwrap()
   136	                .write()
   137	                .insert(file.clone(), FdFlags::empty());
   138	            // Perhaps we should remove the inserted files from the file table if we cannot write
   139	            // the file descriptor back to user space? However, even Linux cannot handle every
   140	            // corner case (https://elixir.bootlin.com/linux/v6.15.2/source/net/core/scm.c#L357).
   141	            writer.write_val::<i32>(&fd)?;
   142	        }
   143	
   144	        Ok(header)
   145	    }
   146	}
   147	
   148	#[derive(Debug)]
   149	struct CredMessage {
   150	    cred: CUserCred,
   151	}
   152	
   153	impl CredMessage {
   154	    fn read_from(header: &CControlHeader, reader: &mut VmReader) -> Result<Self> {
   155	        if header.payload_len() != size_of::<CUserCred>() {
   156	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EINVAL, "the SCM_CREDENTIALS message is invalid");
   157	        }
   158	
   159	        let cred = reader.read_val()?;
   160	
   161	        Ok(Self { cred })
   162	    }
   163	
   164	    fn write_to(&self, writer: &mut VmWriter) -> Result<CControlHeader> {
   165	        let payload_len =
   166	            size_of::<CUserCred>().min(CControlHeader::payload_len_from_total(writer.avail())?);
   167	        if payload_len != size_of::<CUserCred>() {
   168	            warn!("setting MSG_CTRUNC is not supported");
   169	        }
   170	
   171	        let header = CControlHeader::new(
   172	            CSocketOptionLevel::SOL_SOCKET,
   173	            CControlType::SCM_CREDENTIALS as i32,
   174	            payload_len,
   175	        );
   176	        writer.write_val(&header)?;
   177	        writer.write_fallible(&mut VmReader::from(self.cred.as_bytes()))?;
   178	
   179	        Ok(header)
   180	    }
   181	}
   182	
   183	/// Control message types.
   184	///
   185	/// Reference: <https://elixir.bootlin.com/linux/v6.13/source/include/linux/socket.h#L178>.
   186	#[repr(i32)]
   187	#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, TryFromInt, PartialEq, Eq)]
   188	#[expect(non_camel_case_types)]
   189	enum CControlType {
   190	    SCM_RIGHTS = 1,
   260	                return_errno_with_message!(
   261	                    Errno::EPERM,
   262	                    "UNIX sockets in SCM_RIGHTS messages can leak kernel resource"
   263	                )
   264	            }
   265	        }
   266	
   267	        Ok(Self { files, cred })
   268	    }
   269	
   270	    /// Fills the current credentials if there are no credentials.
   271	    pub(super) fn fill_cred(&mut self) {
   272	        if self.cred.is_none() {
   273	            self.cred = Some(SocketCred::<ReadOp>::new_current());
   274	        }
   275	    }
   276	
   277	    /// Generates the control messages from the auxiliary data.
   278	    pub(super) fn generate_control(&mut self, is_pass_cred: bool) -> Vec<ControlMessage> {
   279	        let mut ctrl_msgs = Vec::new();
   280	
   281	        let Self { files, cred } = self;
   282	
   283	        if is_pass_cred {
   284	            let unix_ctrl_msg = UnixControlMessage(Message::Cred(CredMessage {
   285	                cred: cred
   286	                    .as_ref()
   287	                    .map(SocketCred::to_real_c_cred)
   288	                    .unwrap_or_else(CUserCred::new_overflow),
   289	            }));
   290	            ctrl_msgs.push(ControlMessage::Unix(unix_ctrl_msg));
   291	        }
   292	
   293	        if !files.is_empty() {
   294	            let unix_ctrl_msg = UnixControlMessage(Message::Files(FileMessage {
   295	                files: core::mem::take(files),
   296	            }));
   297	            ctrl_msgs.push(ControlMessage::Unix(unix_ctrl_msg));
   298	        }
   299	
   300	        ctrl_msgs
   301	    }
   302	
   303	    /// Returns whether the auxiliary data contains nothing.
   304	    pub(super) fn is_empty(&self) -> bool {
   305	        self.files.is_empty() && self.cred.is_none()
   306	    }
   307	
   308	    /// Returns whether the auxiliary data can be treated as a subset of the other one.
   309	    ///
   310	    /// In stream sockets, we can receive more bytes at once if the current auxiliary data is a
   311	    /// subset of the subsequent auxiliary data.
   312	    pub(super) fn is_subset_of(&self, other: &Self, is_pass_cred: bool) -> bool {
   313	        if !self.files.is_empty() {
   314	            return false;
   315	        }
   316	
   317	        if is_pass_cred
   318	            && self.cred.as_ref().map(SocketCred::to_real_c_cred)
   319	                != other.cred.as_ref().map(SocketCred::to_real_c_cred)
   320	        {
   321	            return false;
   322	        }
   323	
   324	        true
   325	    }
   326	}

    86	    pub(super) fn bind(&self, addr_to_bind: UnixSocketAddr) -> Result<()> {
    87	        let mut addr = self.inner.this_end().addr.lock();
    88	
    89	        if addr.is_some() {
    90	            return addr_to_bind.bind_unnamed();
    91	        }
    92	
    93	        let bound_addr = addr_to_bind.bind()?;
