    98	
    99	    pub(super) fn try_read(
   100	        &self,
   101	        writer: &mut dyn MultiWrite,
   102	        is_seqpacket: bool,
   103	    ) -> Result<(usize, Vec<ControlMessage>)> {
   104	        let is_empty = writer.is_empty();
   105	        if is_empty && !is_seqpacket {
   106	            if self.reader.lock().is_empty() {
   107	                return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EAGAIN, "the channel is empty");
   108	            }
   109	            return Ok((0, Vec::new()));
   110	        }
   111	
   112	        let mut reader = self.reader.lock();
   113	        // `reader.len()` is an `Acquire` operation. So it can guarantee that the `has_aux`
   114	        // check below sees the up-to-date value.
   115	        let no_aux_len = reader.len();
   116	
   117	        let peer_end = self.inner.peer_end();
   118	        let is_pass_cred = self.inner.this_end().is_pass_cred.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
   119	
   120	        // Fast path: There are no auxiliary data to receive.
   121	        if !peer_end.has_aux.load(Ordering::Relaxed) {
   122	            let read_len = self
   123	                .inner
   124	                .read_with(move || reader.read_fallible_with_max_len(writer, no_aux_len))?;
   125	            let ctrl_msgs = if is_pass_cred {
   126	                AuxiliaryData::default().generate_control(is_pass_cred)
   127	            } else {
   128	                Vec::new()
   129	            };
   130	            return Ok((read_len, ctrl_msgs));
   131	        }
   132	
   133	        let mut all_aux = peer_end.all_aux.lock();
   134	        let mut aux_prev_data: Option<AuxiliaryData> = None;
   135	        let mut read_tot_len = 0;
   136	
   137	        let aux_data = loop {
   138	            let read_start = reader.head();
   139	            let (aux_len, aux_front) = if let Some(front) = all_aux.front_mut() {
   140	                if front.start == read_start {
   141	                    ((front.end - read_start).0, Some(front))
   142	                } else {
   143	                    ((front.start - read_start).0, None)
   144	                }
   145	            } else {
   146	                (usize::MAX, None)
   147	            };
   148	
   149	            // Unless the auxiliary data we have already received is a subset of the current
   150	            // auxiliary data, we cannot receive additional bytes.
   151	            if let Some(prev) = aux_prev_data.as_mut() {
   152	                let is_subset = if let Some(front) = aux_front.as_ref() {
   153	                    prev.is_subset_of(&front.data, is_pass_cred)
   154	                } else {
   155	                    prev.is_subset_of(&AuxiliaryData::default(), is_pass_cred)
   156	                };
   157	                if !is_subset {
   158	                    break prev;
   159	                }
   160	            }
   161	
   162	            // Read the payload bytes of the current auxiliary data.
   163	            let read_res = if !is_empty && aux_len > 0 {
   164	                self.inner
   165	                    .read_with(|| reader.read_fallible_with_max_len(writer, aux_len))
   166	            } else {
   167	                Ok(0)
   168	            };
   169	            let read_len = match read_res {
   170	                Ok(read_len) => read_len,
   171	                Err(_) if read_tot_len > 0 => break aux_prev_data.as_mut().unwrap(),
   172	                Err(err) => return Err(err),
   173	            };
   174	            read_tot_len += read_len;
   175	
   176	            // Record the current auxiliary data. Break if the read is incomplete or this is a
   177	            // `SOCK_SEQPACKET` socket.
   178	            if is_seqpacket {
   179	                aux_prev_data = Some(all_aux.pop_front().unwrap().data);
   180	                if read_len < aux_len {
   181	                    warn!("setting MSG_TRUNC is not supported");
   182	                    reader.skip(aux_len - read_len);
   183	                }
   184	                break aux_prev_data.as_mut().unwrap();
   185	            } else if let Some(front) = aux_front {
   186	                if read_len < aux_len {
   187	                    front.start += read_len;
   188	                    break &mut front.data;
   189	                }
   190	                aux_prev_data = Some(all_aux.pop_front().unwrap().data);
   191	            } else {
   192	                aux_prev_data = Some(AuxiliaryData::default());
   193	                if read_len < aux_len {
   194	                    break aux_prev_data.as_mut().unwrap();
   195	                }
   196	            }
   197	        };
   198	
   199	        drop(reader);
   200	
   201	        let ctrl_msgs = aux_data.generate_control(is_pass_cred);
   202	        debug_assert!(is_empty || read_tot_len != 0);
   203	        peer_end
   204	            .has_aux
   205	            .store(!all_aux.is_empty(), Ordering::Relaxed);
   206	
   207	        Ok((read_tot_len, ctrl_msgs))
   208	    }
   209	
   210	    pub(super) fn try_write(
   211	        &self,
   212	        reader: &mut dyn MultiRead,
   213	        aux_data: &mut AuxiliaryData,
   214	        is_seqpacket: bool,
   215	    ) -> Result<usize> {
   216	        let is_empty = reader.is_empty();
   217	        if is_empty {
   218	            if self.inner.is_shutdown() {
   219	                return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EPIPE, "the channel is shut down");
   220	            }
   221	            if !is_seqpacket {
   222	                return Ok(0);
   223	            }
   224	        }
   225	
   226	        if is_seqpacket && reader.sum_lens() >= UNIX_STREAM_DEFAULT_BUF_SIZE {
   227	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EMSGSIZE, "the message is too large");
   228	        }
   229	
   230	        let this_end = self.inner.this_end();
   231	        let need_pass_cred = this_end.is_pass_cred.load(Ordering::Relaxed)
   232	            || self.inner.peer_end().is_pass_cred.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
   233	
   234	        // Fast path: There are no auxiliary data to transmit.
   235	        if aux_data.is_empty() && !is_seqpacket && !need_pass_cred {
   236	            let mut writer = self.writer.lock();
   237	            return self.inner.write_with(move || {
   238	                if is_seqpacket && writer.free_len() < reader.sum_lens() {
   239	                    return Ok(0);
   240	                }
   241	                writer.write_fallible(reader)
   242	            });
   243	        }
   244	
   245	        let mut all_aux = this_end.all_aux.lock();
   246	
   247	        // No matter we succeed later or not, set the flag first to ensure that the auxiliary
   248	        // data are always visible to `try_recv`.
   249	        this_end.has_aux.store(true, Ordering::Relaxed);
   250	
   251	        // Write the payload bytes.
   252	        let (write_start, write_res) = if !is_empty {
   253	            let mut writer = self.writer.lock();
   254	            let write_start = writer.tail();
   255	            let write_res = self.inner.write_with(move || {
   256	                if is_seqpacket && writer.free_len() < reader.sum_lens() {
   257	                    return Ok(0);
   258	                }
   259	                writer.write_fallible(reader)
   260	            });
   261	            (write_start, write_res)
   262	        } else {
   263	            (self.writer.lock().tail(), Ok(0))
   264	        };
   265	        let Ok(write_len) = write_res else {
   266	            this_end
   267	                .has_aux
   268	                .store(!all_aux.is_empty(), Ordering::Relaxed);
   269	            return write_res;
   270	        };
   271	
   272	        if need_pass_cred {
   273	            aux_data.fill_cred();
   274	        }
   275	
   276	        // Store the auxiliary data.
   277	        let aux_range = RangedAuxiliaryData {
   278	            data: core::mem::take(aux_data),
   279	            start: write_start,
   280	            end: write_start + Wrapping(write_len),
   281	        };
   282	        all_aux.push_back(aux_range);
   283	
   284	        Ok(write_len)
   285	    }
   286	
   287	    pub(super) fn shutdown(&self, cmd: SockShutdownCmd) {
   288	        if cmd.shut_read() {
   289	            self.inner.peer_shutdown();
   290	        }
   291	
   292	        if cmd.shut_write() {
   293	            self.inner.shutdown();
   294	        }
   295	    }

   297	    pub(super) fn is_read_shutdown(&self) -> bool {
   298	        self.inner.is_peer_shutdown()
   299	    }

    80	    }

    83	        self.inner.peer_end().addr.lock().clone()
    84	    }

    86	    pub(super) fn bind(&self, addr_to_bind: UnixSocketAddr) -> Result<()> {
    87	        let mut addr = self.inner.this_end().addr.lock();
    88	
    89	        if addr.is_some() {
    90	            return addr_to_bind.bind_unnamed();
    91	        }
    92	
    93	        let bound_addr = addr_to_bind.bind()?;
    94	        *addr = Some(bound_addr);
    95	
    96	        Ok(())
    97	    }
    98	
    99	    pub(super) fn try_read(
   100	        &self,
   101	        writer: &mut dyn MultiWrite,
   102	    ) -> Result<(usize, Vec<ControlMessage>)> {
   103	        if writer.is_empty() {
   104	            if self.reader.lock().is_empty() {
   105	                return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EAGAIN, "the channel is empty");
   106	            }
   107	            return Ok((0, Vec::new()));
   108	        }
   109	
   110	        let mut reader = self.reader.lock();
   111	        // `reader.len()` is an `Acquire` operation. So it can guarantee that the `has_aux`
   112	        // check below sees the up-to-date value.
   113	        let no_aux_len = reader.len();
   114	
   115	        let peer_end = self.inner.peer_end();
   116	        let is_pass_cred = self.inner.this_end().is_pass_cred.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
   117	
   118	        // Fast path: There are no auxiliary data to receive.
   119	        if !peer_end.has_aux.load(Ordering::Relaxed) {
   120	            let read_len = self
   121	                .inner
   122	                .read_with(move || reader.read_fallible_with_max_len(writer, no_aux_len))?;
   123	            let ctrl_msgs = if is_pass_cred {
   124	                AuxiliaryData::default().generate_control(is_pass_cred)
   125	            } else {
   126	                Vec::new()
   127	            };
   128	            return Ok((read_len, ctrl_msgs));
   129	        }
   130	
   131	        let mut all_aux = peer_end.all_aux.lock();
   132	        let mut aux_prev_data: Option<AuxiliaryData> = None;
   133	        let mut read_tot_len = 0;
   134	
   135	        let aux_data = loop {
   136	            let read_start = reader.head();
   137	            let (aux_len, aux_front) = if let Some(front) = all_aux.front_mut() {
   138	                if front.start == read_start {
   139	                    ((front.end - read_start).0, Some(front))
   140	                } else {
   141	                    ((front.start - read_start).0, None)
   142	                }
   143	            } else {
   144	                (usize::MAX, None)
   145	            };
   146	
   147	            // Unless the auxiliary data we have already received is a subset of the current
   148	            // auxiliary data, we cannot receive additional bytes.
   149	            if let Some(prev) = aux_prev_data.as_mut() {
   150	                let is_subset = if let Some(front) = aux_front.as_ref() {
   151	                    prev.is_subset_of(&front.data, is_pass_cred)
   152	                } else {
   153	                    prev.is_subset_of(&AuxiliaryData::default(), is_pass_cred)
   154	                };
   155	                if !is_subset {
   156	                    break prev;
   157	                }
   158	            }
   159	
   160	            // Read the payload bytes of the current auxiliary data.
   161	            let read_res = self
   162	                .inner
   163	                .read_with(|| reader.read_fallible_with_max_len(writer, aux_len));
   164	            let read_len = match read_res {
   165	                Ok(read_len) => read_len,
   166	                Err(_) if read_tot_len > 0 => break aux_prev_data.as_mut().unwrap(),
   167	                Err(err) => return Err(err),
   168	            };
   169	            read_tot_len += read_len;
   170	
   171	            // Record the current auxiliary data. Break if the read is incomplete.
   172	            if let Some(front) = aux_front {
   173	                if read_len < aux_len {
   174	                    front.start += read_len;
   175	                    break &mut front.data;
   176	                }
   177	                aux_prev_data = Some(all_aux.pop_front().unwrap().data);
   178	            } else {
   179	                aux_prev_data = Some(AuxiliaryData::default());
   180	                if read_len < aux_len {
   181	                    break aux_prev_data.as_mut().unwrap();
   182	                }
   183	            }
   184	        };
   185	
   186	        drop(reader);
   187	
   188	        let ctrl_msgs = aux_data.generate_control(is_pass_cred);
   189	        debug_assert_ne!(read_tot_len, 0);
   190	        peer_end
   191	            .has_aux
   192	            .store(!all_aux.is_empty(), Ordering::Relaxed);
   193	
   194	        Ok((read_tot_len, ctrl_msgs))
   195	    }
   196	
   197	    pub(super) fn try_write(
   198	        &self,
   199	        reader: &mut dyn MultiRead,
   200	        aux_data: &mut AuxiliaryData,
   201	    ) -> Result<usize> {
   202	        if reader.is_empty() {
   203	            if self.inner.is_shutdown() {
   204	                return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EPIPE, "the channel is shut down");
   205	            }
   206	            return Ok(0);
   207	        }
   208	
   209	        let this_end = self.inner.this_end();
   210	        let need_pass_cred = this_end.is_pass_cred.load(Ordering::Relaxed)
   211	            || self.inner.peer_end().is_pass_cred.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
   212	
   213	        // Fast path: There are no auxiliary data to transmit.
   214	        if aux_data.is_empty() && !need_pass_cred {
   215	            let mut writer = self.writer.lock();
   216	            return self.inner.write_with(move || writer.write_fallible(reader));
   217	        }
   218	
   219	        let mut all_aux = this_end.all_aux.lock();
   220	
   221	        // No matter we succeed later or not, set the flag first to ensure that the auxiliary
   222	        // data are always visible to `try_recv`.
   223	        this_end.has_aux.store(true, Ordering::Relaxed);
   224	
   225	        // Write the payload bytes.
   226	        let (write_start, write_res) = {
   227	            let mut writer = self.writer.lock();
   228	            let write_start = writer.tail();
   229	            let write_res = self.inner.write_with(move || writer.write_fallible(reader));
   230	            (write_start, write_res)
   231	        };
   232	        let Ok(write_len) = write_res else {
   233	            this_end
   234	                .has_aux
   235	                .store(!all_aux.is_empty(), Ordering::Relaxed);
   236	            return write_res;
   237	        };
   238	
   239	        if need_pass_cred {
   240	            aux_data.fill_cred();
   241	        }
   242	
   243	        // Store the auxiliary data.
   244	        let aux_range = RangedAuxiliaryData {
   245	            data: core::mem::take(aux_data),
   246	            start: write_start,
   247	            end: write_start + Wrapping(write_len),
   248	        };
   249	        all_aux.push_back(aux_range);
   250	
   251	        Ok(write_len)
   252	    }
   253	
   254	    pub(super) fn shutdown(&self, cmd: SockShutdownCmd) {
   255	        if cmd.shut_read() {
   256	            self.inner.peer_shutdown();
   257	        }
   258	
   259	        if cmd.shut_write() {
   260	            self.inner.shutdown();
   261	        }
   262	    }
   263	
   264	    pub(super) fn is_read_shutdown(&self) -> bool {
   265	        self.inner.is_peer_shutdown()
   266	    }
   267	
   268	    pub(super) fn is_write_shutdown(&self) -> bool {
   269	        self.inner.is_shutdown()
   270	    }
   271	
   272	    pub(super) fn set_pass_cred(&self, is_pass_cred: bool) {
   273	        self.inner
   274	            .this_end()
   275	            .is_pass_cred
   276	            .store(is_pass_cred, Ordering::Relaxed);
   277	    }
   278	
   279	    pub(super) fn check_io_events(&self) -> IoEvents {
   280	        let mut events = IoEvents::empty();
   281	
   282	        if !self.reader.lock().is_empty() {
   283	            events |= IoEvents::IN;
   284	        }
   285	
   286	        if !self.writer.lock().is_full() {
   287	            events |= IoEvents::OUT;
   288	        }
   289	
   290	        events
   291	    }
   292	
   293	    pub(super) fn cloned_pollee(&self) -> Pollee {
   294	        self.inner.this_end().state.cloned_pollee()
   295	    }
   296	
   297	    pub(super) fn peer_cred(&self) -> &SocketCred {
   298	        &self.peer_cred
   299	    }
   300	}

+// SPDX-License-Identifier: MPL-2.0
+
+use core::time::Duration;
+
+use super::{poll::do_sys_poll, SyscallReturn};
+use crate::{
+    prelude::*,
+    process::signal::{sig_mask::SigMask, with_sigmask_changed},
+    time::timespec_t,
+};
+
+pub fn sys_ppoll(
+    fds: Vaddr,
+    nfds: u32,
+    timespec_addr: Vaddr,
+    sigmask_addr: Vaddr,
+    sigmask_size: usize,
+    ctx: &Context,
+) -> Result<SyscallReturn> {
+    let user_space = ctx.user_space();
+
+    let timeout = if timespec_addr != 0 {
+        let time_spec = user_space.read_val::<timespec_t>(timespec_addr)?;
+        Some(Duration::try_from(time_spec)?)
+    } else {
+        None
+    };
+
+    if sigmask_addr != 0 {
+        if sigmask_size != size_of::<SigMask>() {
+            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EINVAL, "invalid sigmask size");
+        }
+
+        let sigmask = user_space.read_val::<SigMask>(sigmask_addr)?;
+        with_sigmask_changed(ctx, |_| sigmask, || do_sys_poll(fds, nfds, timeout, ctx))
+    } else {
+        do_sys_poll(fds, nfds, timeout, ctx)
+    }
+
+    // TODO: Write back the remaining time to `timespec_addr`.
+    //
+    // The ppoll system call should write back the remaining time,
+    // yet the function counterpart in libc hides this behavior to
+    // make the API more portable across different UNIX-like OSes.
+    // For the maximized Linux compatibility, we should follow Linux's behavior.
+    // But this cannot be readily achieved given how our internal synchronization primitives
+    // such as `Pause` and `WaitTimeout` work.
+}

    46	// TODO: This interface of this method is error prone.
    47	// The method takes an argument for the current thread to optimize its efficiency.
    48	/// Handle pending signal for current process.
    49	pub fn handle_pending_signal(
    50	    user_ctx: &mut UserContext,
    51	    ctx: &Context,
    52	    syscall_number: Option<usize>,
    53	) {
    54	    let syscall_restart = if let Some(syscall_number) = syscall_number
    55	        && user_ctx.syscall_ret() == -(Errno::ERESTARTSYS as i32) as usize
    56	    {
    57	        // We should never return `ERESTARTSYS` to the userspace.
    58	        user_ctx.set_syscall_ret(-(Errno::EINTR as i32) as usize);
    59	        Some(syscall_number)
    60	    } else {
    61	        None
    62	    };
    63	
    64	    let posix_thread = ctx.posix_thread;
    65	    let current = ctx.process;
    66	
    67	    let signal = {
    68	        let sig_mask = posix_thread.sig_mask().load(Ordering::Relaxed);
    69	        if let Some(signal) = posix_thread.dequeue_signal(&sig_mask) {
    70	            signal
    71	        } else {
    72	            return;
    73	        }
    74	    };
    75	    let sig_num = signal.num();
    76	    trace!("sig_num = {:?}, sig_name = {}", sig_num, sig_num.sig_name());
    77	
    78	    let mut sig_dispositions = current.sig_dispositions().lock();
    79	
    80	    let sig_action = sig_dispositions.get(sig_num);
    81	    trace!("sig action: {:x?}", sig_action);
    82	
    83	    match sig_action {
    84	        SigAction::Ign => {
    85	            trace!("Ignore signal {:?}", sig_num);
    86	        }
    87	        SigAction::User {
    88	            handler_addr,
    89	            flags,
    90	            restorer_addr,
    91	            mask,
    92	        } => {
    93	            if let Some(syscall_number) = syscall_restart
    94	                && flags.contains(SigActionFlags::SA_RESTART)
    95	            {
    96	                #[cfg(target_arch = "x86_64")]
    97	                const SYSCALL_INSTR_LEN: usize = 2; // syscall
    98	                #[cfg(target_arch = "riscv64")]
    99	                const SYSCALL_INSTR_LEN: usize = 4; // ecall
   100	                #[cfg(target_arch = "loongarch64")]
   101	                const SYSCALL_INSTR_LEN: usize = 4; // syscall
   102	
   103	                user_ctx.set_syscall_num(syscall_number);
   104	                user_ctx
   105	                    .set_instruction_pointer(user_ctx.instruction_pointer() - SYSCALL_INSTR_LEN);
   106	            }
   107	
   108	            if flags.contains(SigActionFlags::SA_RESETHAND) {
   109	                // In Linux, SA_RESETHAND corresponds to SA_ONESHOT,
   110	                // which means the user handler will be executed only once and then reset to the default.
   111	                // Refer to https://elixir.bootlin.com/linux/v6.0.9/source/kernel/signal.c#L2761.
   112	                sig_dispositions.set_default(sig_num);
   113	            }
   114	
   115	            drop(sig_dispositions);
   116	            if let Err(e) = handle_user_signal(
   117	                ctx,
   118	                sig_num,
   119	                handler_addr,
   120	                flags,
   121	                restorer_addr,
   122	                mask,
   123	                user_ctx,
   124	                signal.to_info(),
   125	            ) {
   126	                debug!("Failed to handle user signal: {:?}", e);
   127	                // If signal handling fails, the process should be terminated with SIGSEGV.
   128	                // Ref: <https://elixir.bootlin.com/linux/v6.13/source/kernel/signal.c#L3082>
   129	                do_exit_group(TermStatus::Killed(SIGSEGV));
   130	            }
   131	        }
   132	        SigAction::Dfl => {
   133	            drop(sig_dispositions);
   134	
   135	            let sig_default_action = SigDefaultAction::from_signum(sig_num);
   136	            trace!("sig_default_action: {:?}", sig_default_action);
   137	            match sig_default_action {
   138	                SigDefaultAction::Core | SigDefaultAction::Term => {
   139	                    warn!(
   140	                        "{:?}: terminating on signal {}",
   141	                        current.executable_path(),
   142	                        sig_num.sig_name()
   143	                    );
   144	                    // We should exit current here, since we cannot restore a valid status from trap now.
   145	                    do_exit_group(TermStatus::Killed(sig_num));
   146	                }
   147	                SigDefaultAction::Ign => {}
   148	                SigDefaultAction::Stop => ctx.process.stop(sig_num),
   149	                SigDefaultAction::Cont => ctx.process.resume(),
   150	            }
   151	        }
   152	    }
   153	}
   154	
   155	#[expect(clippy::too_many_arguments)]
   156	pub fn handle_user_signal(
   157	    ctx: &Context,
   158	    sig_num: SigNum,
   159	    handler_addr: Vaddr,
   160	    flags: SigActionFlags,
   161	    restorer_addr: Vaddr,
   162	    mut mask: SigMask,
   163	    user_ctx: &mut UserContext,
   164	    sig_info: siginfo_t,
   165	) -> Result<()> {
   166	    debug!("sig_num = {:?}, signame = {}", sig_num, sig_num.sig_name());
   167	    debug!("handler_addr = 0x{:x}", handler_addr);
   168	    debug!("flags = {:?}", flags);
   169	    debug!("restorer_addr = 0x{:x}", restorer_addr);
   170	
   171	    if flags.contains_unsupported_flag() {
   172	        warn!("Unsupported Signal flags: {:?}", flags);
   173	    }
   174	
   175	    if !flags.contains(SigActionFlags::SA_NODEFER) {
   176	        // Add current signal to mask
   177	        mask += sig_num;
   178	    }
   179	
   180	    // Block signals in sigmask when running signal handler
   181	    let old_mask = ctx.posix_thread.sig_mask().load(Ordering::Relaxed);
   182	    ctx.posix_thread
   183	        .sig_mask()
   184	        .store(old_mask + mask, Ordering::Relaxed);
   185	
   186	    // Set up signal stack.
   187	    let mut stack_pointer = if let Some(sp) = use_alternate_signal_stack(ctx.thread_local) {
   188	        sp as u64
   189	    } else {
   190	        // Just use user stack
   191	        user_ctx.stack_pointer() as u64
   192	    };
   193	
   194	    // To avoid corrupting signal stack, we minus 128 first.
   195	    stack_pointer -= 128;
   196	
   197	    let user_space = ctx.user_space();
   198	
   199	    // 1. Write siginfo_t
   200	    stack_pointer -= mem::size_of::<siginfo_t>() as u64;
   201	    user_space.write_val(stack_pointer as _, &sig_info)?;
   202	    let siginfo_addr = stack_pointer;
   203	
   204	    // 2. Write ucontext_t.
   205	
   206	    let mut ucontext = ucontext_t {
   207	        uc_sigmask: mask.into(),
   208	        ..Default::default()
   209	    };
   210	    ucontext.uc_mcontext.copy_user_regs_from(user_ctx);
   211	    let sig_context = ctx.thread_local.sig_context().get();
   212	    if let Some(sig_context_addr) = sig_context {
   213	        ucontext.uc_link = sig_context_addr;
   214	    } else {
   215	        ucontext.uc_link = 0;
   216	    }
   217	
   218	    // Clone and reset the FPU context.
   219	    let fpu_context = ctx.thread_local.fpu().clone_context();
   220	    let fpu_context_bytes = fpu_context.as_bytes();
    50	    pub fn dequeue(&self, blocked: &SigMask) -> Option<Box<dyn Signal>> {
    51	        // Fast path for the common case of no pending signals
    52	        if self.is_empty() {
    53	            return None;
    54	        }
    55	
    56	        let mut queues = self.queues.lock();
    57	        let signal = queues.dequeue(blocked);
    58	        if signal.is_some() {
    59	            self.count.fetch_sub(1, Ordering::Relaxed);
    60	        }
    61	        signal
    62	    }
    63	
    64	    /// Returns the pending signals
    65	    pub fn sig_pending(&self) -> SigSet {
    66	        let queues = self.queues.lock();
    67	        queues.sig_pending()
    68	    }
    69	
    70	    /// Returns whether there's some pending signals that are not blocked
    71	    pub fn has_pending(&self, blocked: SigMask) -> bool {
    72	        if self.is_empty() {
    73	            return false;
    74	        }
    75	        self.queues.lock().has_pending(blocked)
    76	    }

    78	    pub fn register_observer(
    79	        &self,
    80	        observer: Weak<dyn Observer<SigEvents>>,
    81	        filter: SigEventsFilter,
    82	    ) {
    83	        self.subject.register_observer(observer, filter);
    84	    }
    85	
    86	    pub fn unregister_observer(&self, observer: &Weak<dyn Observer<SigEvents>>) {
    87	        self.subject.unregister_observer(observer);
    88	    }

     3	//! Signal sets and atomic masks.
     4	//!
     5	//! A signal set is a bit-set of signals. A signal mask is a set of signals
     6	//! that are blocked from delivery to a thread. An atomic signal mask
     7	//! implementation is provided for shared access to signal masks.
     8	
     9	use core::{
    10	    fmt::LowerHex,
    11	    ops,
    12	    sync::atomic::{AtomicU64, Ordering},
    13	};
    14	
    15	use atomic_integer_wrapper::define_atomic_version_of_integer_like_type;
    16	
    17	use super::{constants::MIN_STD_SIG_NUM, sig_num::SigNum};
    18	use crate::prelude::*;
    19	
    20	/// A signal mask.
    21	///
    22	/// This is an alias to the [`SigSet`]. All the signal in the set are blocked
    23	/// from the delivery to a thread.
    24	pub type SigMask = SigSet;
    25	
    26	/// A bit-set of signals.
    27	///
    28	/// Because that all the signal numbers are in the range of 1 to 64, casting
    29	/// a signal set from `u64` to `SigSet` will always succeed.
    30	#[derive(Debug, Copy, Clone, Default, PartialEq, Eq, Pod)]
    31	#[repr(C)]
    32	pub struct SigSet {
    33	    bits: u64,
    34	}
    35	
    36	impl From<SigNum> for SigSet {
    37	    fn from(signum: SigNum) -> Self {
    38	        let idx = signum.as_u8() - MIN_STD_SIG_NUM;
    39	        Self { bits: 1_u64 << idx }
    40	    }
    41	}
    42	
    43	impl From<u64> for SigSet {
    44	    fn from(bits: u64) -> Self {
    45	        SigSet { bits }
    46	    }
    47	}
    48	
    49	impl From<SigSet> for u64 {
    50	    fn from(set: SigSet) -> u64 {
    51	        set.bits
    52	    }
    53	}
    54	
    55	impl<T: Into<SigSet>> ops::BitAnd<T> for SigSet {
    56	    type Output = Self;
    57	
    58	    fn bitand(self, rhs: T) -> Self {
    59	        SigSet {
    60	            bits: self.bits & rhs.into().bits,
    61	        }
    62	    }
    63	}
    64

   136	    }

   139	        self.bits == 0
   140	    }
   141

   143	        self.bits == !0
   144	    }

   151	        let other = other.into();
   152	        self.bits & other.bits == other.bits
   153	    }
   154

   156	        let other = other.into();

   139	    /// accumulating total bytes read.

   141	    /// If the return value is `Ok(n)`,

   144	    /// # Errors

   189	
   190	impl MultiRead for VmReaderArray<'_> {
   191	    fn read(&mut self, writer: &mut VmWriter<'_, Infallible>) -> Result<usize> {
   192	        let mut total_len = 0;
   193	
   194	        for reader in &mut self.0 {
   195	            let copied_len = reader.read_fallible(writer)?;
   196	            total_len += copied_len;
   197	            if !writer.has_avail() {
   198	                break;
   199	            }
   200	        }
   201	        Ok(total_len)
   202	    }
   203	
   204	    fn sum_lens(&self) -> usize {
   205	        self.0.iter().map(|vm_reader| vm_reader.remain()).sum()
   206	    }
   207	
   208	    fn skip_some(&mut self, mut nbytes: usize) {
   209	        for reader in &mut self.0 {
   210	            let bytes_to_skip = reader.remain().min(nbytes);
   211	            reader.skip(bytes_to_skip);
   212	            nbytes -= bytes_to_skip;
   213	
   214	            if nbytes == 0 {
   215	                return;
   216	            }
   217	        }
   218	    }
   219	}

    55	    pub fn try_recv(&self, writer: &mut dyn MultiWrite) -> Result<usize> {
    56	        let mut connection = self.connection.disable_irq().lock();
    57	        let bytes_read = connection.buffer.read_fallible(writer)?;
    58	        connection.info.done_forwarding(bytes_read);
    59	        self.pollee.invalidate();
    60	
    61	        match bytes_read {
    62	            0 => {
    63	                if !connection.is_peer_requested_shutdown() {
    64	                    return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EAGAIN, "the receive buffer is empty");
    65	                } else {
    66	                    return_errno_with_message!(Errno::ECONNRESET, "the connection is reset");
    67	                }
    68	            }
    69	            bytes_read => Ok(bytes_read),
    70	        }
    71	    }
    72	
    73	    pub fn send(&self, reader: &mut dyn MultiRead, flags: SendRecvFlags) -> Result<usize> {
    74	        let mut connection = self.connection.disable_irq().lock();
    75	        // TODO: Deal with flags
    76	        if !flags.is_all_supported() {
    77	            warn!("unsupported flags: {:?}", flags);
    78	        }
    79	        let buf_len = reader.sum_lens();
    80	        VSOCK_GLOBAL
    81	            .get()
    82	            .unwrap()
    83	            .send(reader, &mut connection.info)?;
    84	
    85	        Ok(buf_len)
    86	    }
    87	
    88	    pub fn should_close(&self) -> bool {
    89	        let connection = self.connection.disable_irq().lock();
    90	        // If buffer is now empty and the peer requested shutdown, finish shutting down the
    91	        // connection.
    92	        connection.is_peer_requested_shutdown() && connection.buffer.is_empty()
    93	    }
    94	
    95	    pub fn is_closed(&self) -> bool {
    96	        let connection = self.connection.disable_irq().lock();
    97	        connection.is_local_shutdown()
    98	    }
    99	
   100	    pub fn shutdown(&self, _cmd: SockShutdownCmd) -> Result<()> {
   101	        // TODO: deal with cmd
   102	        if self.should_close() {
   103	            let mut connection = self.connection.disable_irq().lock();
   104	            if connection.is_local_shutdown() {
   105	                return Ok(());
   106	            }
   107	            let vsockspace = VSOCK_GLOBAL.get().unwrap();
   108	            vsockspace.reset(&connection.info).unwrap();
   109	            connection.set_local_shutdown();
   110	        }
   111	        Ok(())
   112	    }

   114	        let mut connection = self.connection.disable_irq().lock();
   115	        connection.update_for_event(event)

   330	///
   331	/// This trait is added instead of creating a new method in [`Pollee`] because sometimes we do not
   332	/// have access to the internal [`Pollee`], but there is a method that provides the same semantics
   333	/// as [`Pollable::poll`] and we need to perform event-based operations using that method.
   334	pub trait Pollable {
   335	    /// Returns the interesting events now and monitors their occurrence in the future if the
   336	    /// poller is provided.
   337	    ///
   338	    /// This method has the same semantics as [`Pollee::poll_with`].
   339	    fn poll(&self, mask: IoEvents, poller: Option<&mut PollHandle>) -> IoEvents;
   340	
   341	    /// Waits for events and performs event-based operations.
   342	    ///
   343	    /// If a call to `try_op()` succeeds or fails with an error code other than `EAGAIN`, the
   344	    /// method will return whatever the call to `try_op()` returns. Otherwise, the method will wait
   345	    /// for some interesting events specified in `mask` to happen and try again.
   346	    ///
   347	    /// This method will fail with `ETIME` if the timeout is specified and the event does not occur
   348	    /// before the timeout expires.
   349	    ///
   350	    /// The user must ensure that a call to `try_op()` does not fail with `EAGAIN` when the
   351	    /// interesting events occur. However, it is allowed to have spurious `EAGAIN` failures due to
   352	    /// race opitions where the events are consumed by another thread.
   353	    #[track_caller]
   354	    fn wait_events<F, R>(
   355	        &self,
   356	        mask: IoEvents,
   357	        timeout: Option<&Duration>,
   358	        mut try_op: F,
   359	    ) -> Result<R>
   360	    where
   361	        Self: Sized,
   362	        F: FnMut() -> Result<R>,
   363	    {
   364	        // Fast path: Return immediately if the operation gives a result.
   365	        match try_op() {
   366	            Err(err) if err.error() == Errno::EAGAIN => (),
   367	            result => return result,
   368	        }
   369	
   370	        // Fast path: Return immediately if the timeout is zero.
   371	        if timeout.is_some_and(|duration| duration.is_zero()) {
   372	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::ETIME, "the timeout expired");
   373	        }
   374	
   375	        // Create the poller and register to wait for the events.
   376	        let mut poller = Poller::new(timeout);
   377	        if self.poll(mask, Some(poller.as_handle_mut())).is_empty() {
   378	            poller.wait()?;
   379	        }
   380	
   381	        loop {
   382	            // Try again after the event happens.
   383	            match try_op() {
   384	                Err(err) if err.error() == Errno::EAGAIN => (),
   385	                result => return result,
   386	            };
   387	
   388	            // Wait until the next event happens.
   389	            poller.wait()?;
   390	        }
   391	    }
   392	}
   393

   148	        // TODO: Support other flags (e.g., `O_ASYNC`)
   149	        if self.is_nonblocking() {
   150	            StatusFlags::O_NONBLOCK
   151	        } else {
   152	            StatusFlags::empty()
   153	        }
   154	    }
   155	
   156	    fn set_status_flags(&self, new_flags: StatusFlags) -> Result<()> {
   157	        // TODO: Support other flags (e.g., `O_ASYNC`)
   158	        if new_flags.contains(StatusFlags::O_NONBLOCK) {
   159	            self.set_nonblocking(true);
   160	        } else {
   161	            self.set_nonblocking(false);
   162	        }
   163	        Ok(())
   164	    }
   165

   167	        Some(self)
   168	    }

    23	    use crate::{events::IoEvents, prelude::*, process::signal::Pollable};
    24	
    25	    /// Common methods for sockets, but private to the network module.
    26	    ///
    27	    /// These are implementation details of sockets, so shouldn't be accessed outside the network
    28	    /// module. Therefore, the whole trait is sealed.
    29	    pub trait SocketPrivate: Pollable {
    30	        /// Returns whether the socket is in non-blocking mode.
    31	        fn is_nonblocking(&self) -> bool;
    32	
    33	        /// Sets whether the socket is in non-blocking mode.
    34	        fn set_nonblocking(&self, nonblocking: bool);
    35	
    36	        /// Blocks until some events occur to complete I/O operations.
    37	        ///
    38	        /// If the socket is in non-blocking mode and the I/O operations cannot be completed
    39	        /// immediately, this method will fail with [`EAGAIN`] instead of blocking.
    40	        ///
    41	        /// [`EAGAIN`]: crate::error::Errno::EAGAIN
    42	        #[track_caller]
    43	        fn block_on<F, R>(&self, events: IoEvents, mut try_op: F) -> Result<R>
    44	        where
    45	            Self: Sized,
    46	            F: FnMut() -> Result<R>,
    47	        {
    48	            if self.is_nonblocking() {
    49	                try_op()
    50	            } else {
    51	                self.wait_events(events, None, try_op)
    52	            }
    53	        }
    54	    }
    55	}

codex
Validation confirms the main findings: the workflow’s `false || true` expressions erase both explicit matrix opt-outs; the seqpacket implementation accepts zero-length sends while enqueueing metadata without consuming bounded payload space; and `pause_timeout` can miss a signal that arrives before its signal waker is installed, leaving an infinite wait asleep. I’m writing these findings into the skill’s JSON fragments now, then I’ll run deterministic assembly and final verification/consolidation.

# Summary

<!-- SUMMARY -->

## Correctness

### `.github/workflows/test_x86.yml` line 84

> ```diff
>           release: ${{ matrix.release || true }}
> ```

Incorrect defaulting (major): The expression `matrix.release || true` converts an explicitly configured `false` into `true`. As a result, the `syscall-debug` matrix entry at line `46` runs with `release: true`, so the debug-build coverage is silently lost.

**Fix.** Preserve an explicit `false` value and apply `true` only when the matrix field is absent, or add an explicit default to every matrix entry instead of using boolean `||` defaulting.

### `.github/workflows/test_x86.yml` line 85

> ```diff
>           enable_kvm: ${{ matrix.enable_kvm || true }}
> ```

Incorrect defaulting (major): The expression `matrix.enable_kvm || true` converts the `false` value in the `syscall-exfat-multiboot2-nokvm` entry at line `61` into `true`. That matrix job therefore still enables KVM, so the no-KVM integration path is never tested.

**Fix.** Preserve an explicit `false` value and apply `true` only when the matrix field is absent, or add an explicit default to every matrix entry instead of using boolean `||` defaulting.

### `kernel/src/net/socket/unix/stream/connected.rs` line 226

> ```diff
>         if is_seqpacket && reader.sum_lens() >= UNIX_STREAM_DEFAULT_BUF_SIZE {
>             return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EMSGSIZE, "the message is too large");
>         }
> ```

Off by one (major): The maximum message check rejects a payload whose length is exactly `UNIX_STREAM_DEFAULT_BUF_SIZE`, even though the ring buffer has that exact capacity and the write-side check below accepts it when `free_len()` equals the payload length. A valid maximum-sized `SOCK_SEQPACKET` message consequently fails with `EMSGSIZE`.

**Fix.** Use `>` for the limit check, keeping the boundary consistent with the ring-buffer capacity check.

### `kernel/src/process/signal/pause.rs` line 154

> ```diff
>         if let Some(posix_thread) = posix_thread_opt {
>             posix_thread.set_signalled_waker(self.waker());
>             self.wait();
>             posix_thread.clear_signalled_waker();
> ```

Lost wakeup (major): There is no pending-signal check after installing the waker and before calling `self.wait()`. If another thread enqueues an unblocked signal between `pause_timeout`'s earlier setup and `set_signalled_waker`, `wake_signalled_waker` observes `None` and drops the wake; this path then sleeps indefinitely for an infinite timeout even though `has_pending()` is already true.

**Fix.** After installing the waker, check `posix_thread.has_pending()` before sleeping, or use the same cancellation loop as `pause_until_or_timeout_impl` so the check and wait are protected by the installed waker.

## Security

### `kernel/src/net/socket/unix/stream/connected.rs` line 282

> ```diff
>         let aux_range = RangedAuxiliaryData {
>             data: core::mem::take(aux_data),
>             start: write_start,
>             end: write_start + Wrapping(write_len),
>         };
>         all_aux.push_back(aux_range);
> ```

Unbounded kernel allocation (major): A zero-length `SOCK_SEQPACKET` send reaches this `push_back` without advancing the bounded ring buffer, so an unprivileged peer can repeatedly call `sendmsg` with an empty payload and optional `SCM_RIGHTS` data. Each call consumes another `RangedAuxiliaryData` entry, and `SCM_RIGHTS` entries retain references to files, with no queue or in-flight-resource limit; the receiver can therefore be forced to accumulate unbounded kernel memory and file references.

**Fix.** Charge auxiliary metadata and zero-length packets against a bounded per-connection quota before enqueueing them, and enforce an in-flight file-reference limit for `SCM_RIGHTS`; alternatively reject ancillary-data packets that cannot consume bounded payload space.

 # Summary
 
-<!-- SUMMARY -->
+The reviewed files define the x86 test matrix, UNIX stream/seqpacket buffering with ancillary data, and signal-aware waits. The socket code has useful boundary tests for zero-length seqpacket messages and stream ancillary data, and the lock order is documented near `Inner::all_aux`.
+
+The highest-risk issue is the `pause_timeout` lost-wakeup race, which can leave an otherwise interruptible infinite wait asleep after a signal arrives. The UNIX seqpacket path also permits unbounded metadata accumulation because zero-length packets do not consume ring-buffer capacity; with `SCM_RIGHTS`, this retains file references. The CI matrix has two configuration regressions that silently turn off the debug and no-KVM coverage, and the seqpacket size check rejects the exact buffer-capacity boundary.
+
+The two CI findings share one fix: preserve explicit `false` matrix values while applying defaults only to absent fields. Add regression coverage for signal arrival during poller setup, zero-length ancillary seqpacket sends under backpressure, and the exact maximum packet size. Repository tests were not run during this review.

date: 2026-08-10

# Summary

The reviewed files define the x86 test matrix, UNIX stream/seqpacket buffering with ancillary data, and signal-aware waits. The socket code has useful boundary tests for zero-length seqpacket messages and stream ancillary data, and the lock order is documented near `Inner::all_aux`.

The highest-risk issue is the `pause_timeout` lost-wakeup race, which can leave an otherwise interruptible infinite wait asleep after a signal arrives. The UNIX seqpacket path also permits unbounded metadata accumulation because zero-length packets do not consume ring-buffer capacity; with `SCM_RIGHTS`, this retains file references. The CI matrix has two configuration regressions that silently turn off the debug and no-KVM coverage, and the seqpacket size check rejects the exact buffer-capacity boundary.

The two CI findings share one fix: preserve explicit `false` matrix values while applying defaults only to absent fields. Add regression coverage for signal arrival during poller setup, zero-length ancillary seqpacket sends under backpressure, and the exact maximum packet size. Repository tests were not run during this review.

## Correctness

### `.github/workflows/test_x86.yml` line 84

> ```diff
>           release: ${{ matrix.release || true }}
> ```

Incorrect defaulting (major): The expression `matrix.release || true` converts an explicitly configured `false` into `true`. As a result, the `syscall-debug` matrix entry at line `46` runs with `release: true`, so the debug-build coverage is silently lost.

**Fix.** Shared with the other CI boolean-defaulting finding: preserve an explicit `false` value and apply `true` only when the matrix field is absent, or add an explicit default to every matrix entry instead of using boolean `||` defaulting.

### `.github/workflows/test_x86.yml` line 85

> ```diff
>           enable_kvm: ${{ matrix.enable_kvm || true }}
> ```

Incorrect defaulting (major): The expression `matrix.enable_kvm || true` converts the `false` value in the `syscall-exfat-multiboot2-nokvm` entry at line `61` into `true`. That matrix job therefore still enables KVM, so the no-KVM integration path is never tested.

**Fix.** Shared with the other CI boolean-defaulting finding: preserve an explicit `false` value and apply `true` only when the matrix field is absent, or add an explicit default to every matrix entry instead of using boolean `||` defaulting.

### `kernel/src/net/socket/unix/stream/connected.rs` line 226

> ```diff
>         if is_seqpacket && reader.sum_lens() >= UNIX_STREAM_DEFAULT_BUF_SIZE {
>             return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EMSGSIZE, "the message is too large");
>         }
> ```

Off by one (major): The maximum message check rejects a payload whose length is exactly `UNIX_STREAM_DEFAULT_BUF_SIZE`, even though the ring buffer has that exact capacity and the write-side check below accepts it when `free_len()` equals the payload length. A valid maximum-sized `SOCK_SEQPACKET` message consequently fails with `EMSGSIZE`.

**Fix.** Use `>` for the limit check, keeping the boundary consistent with the ring-buffer capacity check.

### `kernel/src/process/signal/pause.rs` line 154

> ```diff
>         if let Some(posix_thread) = posix_thread_opt {
>             posix_thread.set_signalled_waker(self.waker());
>             self.wait();
>             posix_thread.clear_signalled_waker();
> ```

Lost wakeup (major): There is no pending-signal check after installing the waker and before calling `self.wait()`. If another thread enqueues an unblocked signal between `pause_timeout`'s earlier setup and `set_signalled_waker`, `wake_signalled_waker` observes `None` and drops the wake; this path then sleeps indefinitely for an infinite timeout even though `has_pending()` is already true.

**Fix.** After installing the waker, check `posix_thread.has_pending()` before sleeping, or use the same cancellation loop as `pause_until_or_timeout_impl` so the check and wait are protected by the installed waker.

## Security

### `kernel/src/net/socket/unix/stream/connected.rs` line 282

> ```diff
>         let aux_range = RangedAuxiliaryData {
>             data: core::mem::take(aux_data),
>             start: write_start,
>             end: write_start + Wrapping(write_len),
>         };
>         all_aux.push_back(aux_range);
> ```

Unbounded kernel allocation (major): A zero-length `SOCK_SEQPACKET` send reaches this `push_back` without advancing the bounded ring buffer, so an unprivileged peer can repeatedly call `sendmsg` with an empty payload and optional `SCM_RIGHTS` data. Each call consumes another `RangedAuxiliaryData` entry, and `SCM_RIGHTS` entries retain references to files, with no queue or in-flight-resource limit; the receiver can therefore be forced to accumulate unbounded kernel memory and file references.

**Fix.** Charge auxiliary metadata and zero-length packets against a bounded per-connection quota before enqueueing them, and enforce an in-flight file-reference limit for `SCM_RIGHTS`; alternatively reject ancillary-data packets that cannot consume bounded payload space.

 # Summary
 
-<!-- SUMMARY -->
+The reviewed files define the x86 test matrix, UNIX stream/seqpacket buffering with ancillary data, and signal-aware waits. The socket code has useful boundary tests for zero-length seqpacket messages and stream ancillary data, and the lock order is documented near `Inner::all_aux`.
+
+The highest-risk issue is the `pause_timeout` lost-wakeup race, which can leave an otherwise interruptible infinite wait asleep after a signal arrives. The UNIX seqpacket path also permits unbounded metadata accumulation because zero-length packets do not consume ring-buffer capacity; with `SCM_RIGHTS`, this retains file references. The CI matrix has two configuration regressions that silently turn off the debug and no-KVM coverage, and the seqpacket size check rejects the exact buffer-capacity boundary.
+
+The two CI findings share one fix: preserve explicit `false` matrix values while applying defaults only to absent fields. Add regression coverage for signal arrival during poller setup, zero-length ancillary seqpacket sends under backpressure, and the exact maximum packet size. Repository tests were not run during this review.

---
name: aster-code-review
description: Review code against Asterinas's persona-keyed coding guidelines and write a Markdown review file. Use when asked to review a Git change (diff mode) or a set of target files (files mode) for defects, or inside a write-test-review loop.
---

# aster-code-review

Review code against Asterinas's coding guidelines
— the persona-keyed pages under `book/src/to-contribute/coding-guidelines/`
— and write one Markdown review file.
The review is **recall-first**: miss as few real defects as possible.
It makes both objective calls (undeniable bugs) and subjective calls (guideline violations, each grounded in a cited rule).

There are two review modes, both **anchored at the current checkout (HEAD)**:

- **`diff <base>`** — a Git change:
  the **commit series** the branch adds over its merge-base with `<base>`
  — each commit's message *and* its diff,
  so the review covers per-commit intent and commit hygiene, not just the net change.
  (Commit first; uncommitted edits are not reviewed in diff mode.)
- **`files <path[:lines] ...>`**
  — target code: the working-tree contents of the named files,
  optionally narrowed to line ranges.

To review at a specific commit, check it out first (it becomes HEAD).

This skill is agent-agnostic (Claude Code and Codex).
Only step 3's spawn primitive differs between agents;
everything else is identical.

## Interface

However the skill is triggered,
it receives ONE raw argument string that begins with a mode word:

```
diff   <base>              <output> [--overwrite] [--per-persona-context=auto|yes|no]
files  <path[:lines] ...>  <output> [--overwrite] [--per-persona-context=auto|yes|no]
```

- `<base>` (diff) — **required**,
  any ref or SHA;
  reviews the commit series `merge-base(<base>, HEAD)..HEAD` (each commit's message + diff).
- `<path[:lines] ...>` (files) — **required**, one or more targets in the working tree.
  A target is a path, optionally `path:N-M` or `path:N-M,K-L` (1-based, inclusive);
  repeat a path to add ranges.
  Wrap a path in double quotes to allow spaces or colons (`"a: b.rs":10-20`).
- `<output>` — **required, last positional** (`cp src... dest` style);
  refuse to overwrite unless `--overwrite`.
- `--overwrite` — replace the output file if it already exists.
- `--per-persona-context` — `yes`: fan out one isolated agent per persona (best recall).
  `no`: one combined agent reviews all personas (cheaper, lower recall).
  `auto` (default) currently resolves to `yes`;
  it will become a benchmark-driven heuristic.
  Controls step 3 only.

**Do not pre-split the argument string yourself.**
Pass it verbatim, as a single quoted argument,
to `resolve_target.sh` (step 1)
— the script self-tokenizes (whitespace-split except inside double quotes),
which keeps the parse deterministic and identical across agents.

## Pipeline

Run these steps in order.
Steps 1 and 5 are deterministic scripts;
steps 6–8 use the model.

The helper scripts live in the skill's own `scripts/` directory,
at `.agents/skills/aster-code-review/scripts/` inside the repository under review.
**Invoke every script by its absolute path**
— the working directory is the code under review (the repo root), *not* the skill directory,
so a bare `scripts/…` is not found (notably on Codex, which runs commands from the repo root; do not `cd` into the skill dir either — `resolve_target.sh` needs the repo as its cwd).
Shells do not persist between commands,
so **set `SKILL` at the start of each command that uses it** (or inline the `$(git rev-parse …)`):

```sh
SKILL="$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)/.agents/skills/aster-code-review"; "$SKILL/scripts/resolve_target.sh" '<raw args>'
```

The steps below write `$SKILL/scripts/…` as shorthand for that absolute path.

1. **Resolve the target.**
   `"$SKILL/scripts/resolve_target.sh" '<raw args>'` prints the canonical review input
   — the commit series (each commit's message + diff) in `diff` mode,
   annotated file excerpts in `files` mode;
   save it to a temp file.
   Run it again with `--meta` first (`"$SKILL/scripts/resolve_target.sh" --meta '<raw args>'`) to get
   `mode=`, `base=`/`files=`, `head=`, `branch=`, `output=`, `overwrite=`, `per_persona_context=`;
   add `date=` (today) and an optional `title=` to make the meta file.
   Pass the raw argument string as a single quoted argument
   so the script's tokenizer sees it intact.
2. **Activate personas.**
   Pick which of the five personas run,
   from the reviewed paths — changed paths in `diff` mode,
   named paths in `files` mode (see *Activation*).
3. **Fan out.**
   Spawn the persona passes (see *Spawning*).
   With `per_persona_context` = `yes` or `auto` (the default),
   run **one isolated PASS per activated persona** — best recall.
   With `no`, run **one combined PASS** over all activated personas in a single context
   — cheaper, lower recall.
   Build each pass's prompt deterministically with `"$SKILL/scripts/build_pass_prompt.sh" <input-file> <persona>...`
   — one persona for fan-out, all activated personas for combined
   — and spawn the sub-agent with **that exact text**.
   Each pass returns a JSON array of comments;
   file each under its persona's `<fragdir>/<persona>.json` (the comment's `persona` field says which),
   so step 5 is unchanged.
4. **Collect** the per-persona JSON fragments.
5. **Assemble.**
   `"$SKILL/scripts/assemble_review.sh" [--overwrite] <meta> <fragdir> <output>` performs the deterministic merge (group by persona in fixed order, sort by file→line, drop exact duplicates *within a persona*, write frontmatter, leave a `<!-- SUMMARY -->` placeholder).
   Pass `--overwrite` only if the user gave it;
   otherwise the script refuses to clobber an existing `<output>`.
6. **Verify** (see *Verification*).
7. **Consolidate** (see *Consolidation*).
8. **Summary.**
   Replace `<!-- SUMMARY -->` with a constructive summary over the final comments:
   what the code does well, the top issues ranked by severity,
   any structural recommendations.

## Activation

A persona runs unless the reviewed paths *provably* contain nothing in its remit (reviewed paths = changed paths in `diff` mode, named paths in `files` mode):

- **maintainability, development, security** — any reviewed code.
- **hardware** — a reviewed path is assembly (`*.S`, `*.asm`),
  an architecture directory (`.../arch/...`), or contains `asm!` / `global_asm!`.
- **documentation** — a reviewed path is `book/`, any `*.md`,
  syscall-coverage files (`*.scml`), or a user-facing API surface (a syscall or kernel parameter).

Activation is path-based and deterministic;
do not use a model to triage (a wrongly-skipped persona is a silent recall hole).

## Pass contract

The shared reviewer contract
— the review methodology and the JSON comment schema every pass must follow
— lives in [`scripts/pass_contract.md`](scripts/pass_contract.md),
inlined as the **stable head** of every pass prompt by `build_pass_prompt.sh` (step 3).
Do not paraphrase it into the prompt yourself:
retyping it breaks the byte-identical prefix the prompt cache relies on (see [`execution_model.md`](spec/execution_model.md)).
A pass reads only the persona block(s) it is given (selective exposure),
reviews the **REVIEW INPUT** at the foot of the prompt,
and returns a JSON array of comments per that schema.

## Spawning a pass

Build every pass prompt with `"$SKILL/scripts/build_pass_prompt.sh" <input-file> <persona>...`
and spawn the sub-agent with its **exact** output
— only a script keeps the prompt's stable head (contract + guideline) byte-identical across passes and reviews,
which is what lets the prompt cache reuse it (see [`execution_model.md`](spec/execution_model.md)).

**Default (`per_persona_context` = `yes`/`auto`)**
— one pass per activated persona,
each in a CLEAN context with only its own persona block (selective exposure):

- **Claude Code** — spawn a Task sub-agent per persona.
- **Codex** — run `codex exec "<build_pass_prompt.sh output>"` per persona
  (pass the built PROMPT TEXT as the argument).

**Never spawn a pass by re-running `aster_code_review.sh` / `run_agent.sh`,
nor by re-issuing the skill's own arguments** (e.g. `codex exec … diff <base> <out>`).
A pass is a *reviewer* invocation whose input is the `build_pass_prompt.sh` text
— not another run of this skill.
Re-entering the launcher spawns another orchestrator that spawns another … , an infinite fork bomb;
the launcher now refuses it (`ACR_AGENT_RUNNING`).

Passes are independent;
order does not matter.
Collect each pass's JSON.

**Combined (`per_persona_context` = `no`)**
— build ONE prompt with all activated personas (`"$SKILL/scripts/build_pass_prompt.sh" <input> <p1> <p2> ...`)
and run a single pass;
it reviews through every lens at once and tags each comment with its `persona`.
The input and spawn overhead are paid once,
so it is cheaper
— but a larger shared context dilutes focus,
so it finds fewer defects.
The default stays fan-out:
recall comes first,
and `no` is an explicit, opt-in concession.

## Verification (step 6)

For each comment, isolate the key premise it rests on
— especially an external-system fact (Linux/POSIX behaviour, the System V ABI, Rust semantics).
Try to **refute** it:
re-read the cited code and consult an authoritative source.
Assign a verdict:

- **confirmed** — keep the comment unchanged.
- **uncertain** — keep it, but prefix `problem` with `(unverified) `.
- **refuted** — remove the comment,
  and append it to a `## Retracted by verification` list at the foot of the file with a one-line reason.

Remove **only** on confident refutation;
an unsure check is `uncertain`, not `refuted`.
This is the only step that may remove a comment, and only false positives.

## Consolidation (step 7)

Find clusters of comments that share one root cause or one fix
(e.g. several manual lock/unlock pairs that all want RAII).
For each cluster, write a single unified fix
and repoint each member's `**Fix.**` at it ("Shared with the other `raii` comments: …").
**Never remove a comment**
— every symptom stays at its own location.

## Output format

The assembled file is:
frontmatter (`date`, `mode`, `base` or `files`, `head`, `branch`, optional `title`),
a `# Summary`,
then a `## <section>` per persona in the order **Maintainability, Correctness, Security, Hardware, Documentation** (Correctness is the Development persona's section).
Each comment is a `### \`file\` line N` subsection:
a quoted ` ```diff ` snippet,
then a prose line `` `<grounding>` (<severity>): <problem> ``,
then a `**Fix.**` paragraph.
Do not restructure what the script emits;
only fill the summary and apply the verify/consolidate edits.

--- maintainability stderr
    fn pause_until_or_timeout_impl<F, R>(
        &self,
        cond: F,
        timeout: Option<&ManagedTimeout>,
    ) -> Result<R>
    where
        F: FnMut() -> Option<R>,
    {
        // No fast paths for `Waiter`. If the caller wants a fast path, it should do so _before_
        // the waiter is created.

        let Some(posix_thread) = self
            .task()
            .as_thread()
            .and_then(|thread| thread.as_posix_thread())
        else {
            return self.wait_until_or_timeout_cancelled(cond, || Ok(()), timeout);
        };

`module-docs` (minor): `connected.rs` defines the connected Unix stream socket state, buffers, auxiliary-data tracking, and peer credentials, but the module has no `//!` overview. Readers have to infer how `Connected` relates to `socket.rs`, `init.rs`, and `listener.rs` from the implementation.

**Fix.** Add a short `//!` module doc after the SPDX line that describes the connected-state role, the key `Connected` type, and how it is entered from the surrounding Unix stream socket modules.

### `kernel/src/net/socket/unix/stream/connected.rs` line 99

> ```diff
>     pub(super) fn try_read(
>         &self,
>         writer: &mut dyn MultiWrite,
>         is_seqpacket: bool,
>     ) -> Result<(usize, Vec<ControlMessage>)> {
> ...
>     pub(super) fn try_write(
>         &self,
>         reader: &mut dyn MultiRead,
>         aux_data: &mut AuxiliaryData,
>         is_seqpacket: bool,
>     ) -> Result<usize> {
> ```

`no-bool-args` (major): `try_read()` and `try_write()` take `is_seqpacket: bool` to select stream versus seqpacket behavior, and the flag drives several branches inside both functions. Call sites pass a raw boolean, so readers must inspect the callee to know which socket semantics are being selected.

**Fix.** Replace `is_seqpacket` with a small mode enum such as `UnixStreamMode::{Stream, Seqpacket}`, or split the mode-specific paths into separate helpers like `try_read_stream()` and `try_read_seqpacket()`.

### `kernel/src/process/signal/pause.rs` line 15

> ```diff

> ```

`rfc1574-summary` (minor): The first doc line for the `Pause` trait is a full sentence instead of the noun-phrase summary expected for types and traits.

**Fix.** Rewrite the summary line as a noun phrase, for example: `/// Signal-aware waiting helpers for [`Waiter`] and [`WaitQueue`].`

### `kernel/src/process/signal/pause.rs` line 204

> ```diff
>     fn pause_timeout(&self, _timeout: &TimeoutExt<'_>) -> Result<()> {
>         panic!("`pause_timeout` can only be used on `Waiter`");
>     }
> ```

`least-surprise` (major): `Pause::pause_timeout()` is part of the shared `Pause` trait for both `Waiter` and `WaitQueue`, but the `WaitQueue` implementation unconditionally panics. That makes the trait API advertise a method that one implementor cannot support.

**Fix.** Either implement `pause_timeout()` for `WaitQueue`, or move it into a separate extension trait implemented only for `Waiter`, leaving `Pause` with only methods both `Waiter` and `WaitQueue` can satisfy.

## Correctness

### `.github/workflows/test_x86.yml` line 84

> ```diff

Incorrect test configuration (major): When `matrix.release` is explicitly `false`, `false || true` evaluates to `true`, so `syscall-debug` and `syscall-ext2-iommu-debug` run with `RELEASE=1` instead of debug settings. The same expression is repeated for the gVisor step at line `99`.

**Fix.** Pass `${{ matrix.release }}` directly and set `release: true` explicitly on matrix entries that need release mode.

### `.github/workflows/test_x86.yml` line 85

> ```diff
>           release: ${{ matrix.release || true }}
>           enable_kvm: ${{ matrix.enable_kvm || true }}
> ```

Incorrect test configuration (major): When `matrix.enable_kvm` is explicitly `false`, `false || true` evaluates to `true`, so `syscall-exfat-multiboot2-nokvm` runs with KVM enabled and does not exercise its intended no-KVM path. The same expression is repeated for the gVisor step at line `100`.

**Fix.** Pass `${{ matrix.enable_kvm }}` directly and set `enable_kvm: true` explicitly on matrix entries that need KVM.

### `kernel/src/net/socket/unix/stream/connected.rs` line 202

> ```diff
>         debug_assert!(is_empty || read_tot_len != 0);
> ```

Reachable panic (major): A valid zero-length `SOCK_SEQPACKET` packet makes `read_tot_len` remain `0` even when the receive buffer is non-empty, so this `debug_assert!` panics in debug builds after `recv()` consumes such a packet.

**Fix.** Allow a successfully dequeued zero-length `SOCK_SEQPACKET` packet in the invariant, or track packet consumption separately from payload length, and add a debug-build regression test.

### `kernel/src/net/socket/unix/stream/connected.rs` line 263

> ```diff
>         } else {
>             (self.writer.lock().tail(), Ok(0))
>         };
> ```

Missing readiness notification (major): A zero-length `SOCK_SEQPACKET` send skips `Endpoint::write_with`, so the peer receives no `IoEvents::IN` notification. `check_io_events()` also checks only whether the payload ring buffer is non-empty, while the zero-length packet exists only in `all_aux`; a peer already blocked in `recv` or `poll` can sleep indefinitely.

**Fix.** After enqueueing a zero-length packet, notify the peer's input `Pollee`, and make `check_io_events()` report `IN` when the pending auxiliary-data queue contains a packet.

### `kernel/src/process/signal/pause.rs` line 154

> ```diff
>             posix_thread.set_signalled_waker(self.waker());
>             self.wait();
>             posix_thread.clear_signalled_waker();
> ```

Lost wakeup (major): `pause_timeout` checks `has_pending()` only after `self.wait()`. If a signal is already pending before `set_signalled_waker()` runs, no wakeup is generated for this waiter; with `TimeoutExt::Never` the thread sleeps indefinitely, and with a finite timeout it waits until expiry before returning `EINTR`.

**Fix.** Check `posix_thread.has_pending()` before sleeping and keep the pending-signal check in the same cancellation loop used by `pause_until_or_timeout_cancelled`.

### `kernel/src/process/signal/pause.rs` line 205

> ```diff
>     fn pause_timeout(&self, _timeout: &TimeoutExt<'_>) -> Result<()> {
>         panic!("`pause_timeout` can only be used on `Waiter`");
>     }
> ```

Reachable panic (minor): `WaitQueue::pause_timeout()` is callable through the public `Pause` trait but unconditionally invokes `panic!`, so a safe caller can trigger a kernel panic by calling the method on a `WaitQueue`.

**Fix.** Remove `pause_timeout` from the shared trait and expose it only for `Waiter`, or return an ordinary error such as `EOPNOTSUPP` instead of panicking.

## Security

### `.github/workflows/test_x86.yml` line 15

> ```diff
>     container:
>       image: asterinas/asterinas:0.16.0-20250802
>       options: --device=/dev/kvm --privileged
> ```

Privileged CI execution (major): The `pull_request` workflow executes contributor-controlled `./.github/actions/test` inside a container started with `--privileged` and access to `/dev/kvm`. A malicious pull request can use this to attack the runner host and compromise the workflow's `GITHUB_TOKEN`.

**Fix.** Run pull-request tests without `--privileged` and host-device access. Gate KVM-dependent tests behind a trusted workflow or explicit approval.

### `kernel/src/net/socket/unix/stream/connected.rs` line 282

> ```diff
>         } else {
>             (self.writer.lock().tail(), Ok(0))
>         };
> 
>         // Store the auxiliary data.
>         let aux_range = RangedAuxiliaryData {
>             data: core::mem::take(aux_data),
>             start: write_start,
>             end: write_start + Wrapping(write_len),
>         };
>         all_aux.push_back(aux_range);
> ```

Unbounded memory growth (major): For `SOCK_SEQPACKET`, an empty `reader` produces `write_len == 0`, but this path still appends a `RangedAuxiliaryData` entry. Repeated zero-payload `sendmsg` calls with `SCM_RIGHTS`, or with `SO_PASSCRED` enabled, never consume ring-buffer space while `all_aux` retains each record and its file references, allowing unbounded kernel-memory exhaustion.

**Fix.** Bound the queued auxiliary-record metadata independently of payload capacity, or reject zero-length packets with control data once the metadata queue reaches a limit.

### `kernel/src/process/signal/pause.rs` line 153

> ```diff
>         if let Some(posix_thread) = posix_thread_opt {
>             posix_thread.set_signalled_waker(self.waker());
>             self.wait();
>             posix_thread.clear_signalled_waker();
>         }
> ```

Lost wake-up (major): `pause_timeout()` does not check `posix_thread.has_pending()` after installing the waker and before calling `self.wait()`. If a deliverable signal is already pending when this path starts, installing the waker does not generate a wake-up, so an infinite-timeout `poll()` or `ppoll()` can sleep indefinitely despite the signal.

**Fix.** Check `posix_thread.has_pending()` after registering the waker and before waiting, or use the cancellation loop from `pause_until_or_timeout_impl` so already-pending signals cannot be missed.

### `kernel/src/process/signal/pause.rs` line 219

> ```diff
>     let old_mask = sig_mask.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
>     sig_mask.store(mask_op(old_mask), Ordering::Relaxed);
> ```

`validate-at-boundaries` (major): `with_sigmask_changed()` stores the caller-provided mask unchanged. `pselect6` and `ppoll` pass user-supplied masks through this helper, so a process can include `SIGKILL` or `SIGSTOP`; `SigQueues::dequeue()` then treats those signals as blocked while the syscall sleeps, allowing the process to evade an otherwise fatal or stopping signal indefinitely.

**Fix.** Remove `SIGKILL` and `SIGSTOP` from the temporary mask before storing it, or enforce that normalization in each syscall boundary before calling `with_sigmask_changed()`.

**Fix.** Update the Linux Compatibility syscall coverage page and its `.scml` file for `setsockopt(2)`, `sendmsg(2)`, and `recvmsg(2)`, documenting `SO_PASSCRED` and `SCM_CREDENTIALS`.

    11	    thread::AsThread,
    12	    time::wait::{ManagedTimeout, TimeoutExt},
    13	};
    14	
    15	/// `Pause` is an extension trait to make [`Waiter`] and [`WaitQueue`] signal aware.
    16	///
    17	/// The original methods of `Waiter` and `WaitQueue` only allow a thread
    18	/// to wait (via `wait`) until it is woken up (via `wake_up`, `wake_one`, or `wake_all`)
    19	/// or a condition is met (via `wait_until`).
    20	/// The `WaitTimeout` extension trait grants the extra ability
    21	/// to wait until a timeout (via `wait_until_or_timeout`).
    22	/// On top of `WaitTimeout`, this `Pause` trait provides the `pause`-family methods,
    23	/// which are similar to the `wait`-family methods except that the methods also return
    24	/// when the waiting thread is interrupted by a POSIX signal.
    25	/// When this happens, the `pause`-family methods return `Err(EINTR)`.
    26	pub trait Pause: WaitTimeout {
    27	    /// Pauses until the condition is met or a signal interrupts.
    28	    ///
    29	    /// # Errors
    30	    ///
    31	    /// This method will return an error with [`EINTR`] if a signal is received before the
    32	    /// condition is met.
    33	    ///
    34	    /// [`EINTR`]: crate::error::Errno::EINTR
    35	    #[track_caller]
    36	    fn pause_until<F, R>(&self, cond: F) -> Result<R>
    37	    where
    38	        F: FnMut() -> Option<R>,
    39	    {
    40	        self.pause_until_or_timeout_impl(cond, None)
    41	    }
    42	
    43	    /// Pauses until the condition is met, the timeout is reached, or a signal interrupts.
    44	    ///
    45	    /// # Errors
    46	    ///
    47	    /// Before the condition is met, this method will return an error with
    48	    ///  - [`EINTR`] if a signal is received;
    49	    ///  - [`ETIME`] if the timeout is reached.
    50	    ///
    51	    /// [`ETIME`]: crate::error::Errno::ETIME
    52	    /// [`EINTR`]: crate::error::Errno::EINTR
    53	    #[track_caller]
    54	    fn pause_until_or_timeout<'a, F, T, R>(&self, mut cond: F, timeout: T) -> Result<R>
    55	    where
    56	        F: FnMut() -> Option<R>,
    57	        T: Into<TimeoutExt<'a>>,
    58	    {
    59	        let timeout = timeout.into();
    60	        let timeout_inner = match timeout.check_expired() {
    61	            Ok(inner) => inner,
    62	            Err(err) => return cond().ok_or(err),
    63	        };
    64	
    65	        self.pause_until_or_timeout_impl(cond, timeout_inner)
    66	    }
    67	
    68	    /// Pauses until the condition is met, the timeout is reached, or a signal interrupts.
    69	    ///
    70	    /// # Errors
    71	    ///
    72	    /// Before the condition is met, this method will return an error with
    73	    ///  - [`EINTR`] if a signal is received;
    74	    ///  - [`ETIME`] if the timeout is reached.
    75	    ///
    76	    /// [`ETIME`]: crate::error::Errno::ETIME
    77	    /// [`EINTR`]: crate::error::Errno::EINTR
    78	    #[doc(hidden)]
    79	    #[track_caller]
    80	    fn pause_until_or_timeout_impl<F, R>(
    81	        &self,
    82	        cond: F,
    83	        timeout: Option<&ManagedTimeout>,
    84	    ) -> Result<R>
    85	    where
    86	        F: FnMut() -> Option<R>;
    87	
    88	    /// Pauses until the thread is woken up, the timeout is reached, or a signal interrupts.
    89	    ///
    90	    /// # Errors
    91	    ///
    92	    /// This method will return an error with
    93	    ///  - [`EINTR`] if a signal is received;
    94	    ///  - [`ETIME`] if the timeout is reached.
    95	    ///
    96	    /// [`ETIME`]: crate::error::Errno::ETIME
    97	    /// [`EINTR`]: crate::error::Errno::EINTR
    98	    #[track_caller]
    99	    fn pause_timeout(&self, timeout: &TimeoutExt<'_>) -> Result<()>;
   100	}
   101	
   102	impl Pause for Waiter {
   103	    fn pause_until_or_timeout_impl<F, R>(
   104	        &self,
   105	        cond: F,
   106	        timeout: Option<&ManagedTimeout>,
   107	    ) -> Result<R>
   108	    where
   109	        F: FnMut() -> Option<R>,
   110	    {
   111	        // No fast paths for `Waiter`. If the caller wants a fast path, it should do so _before_
   112	        // the waiter is created.
   113	
   114	        let Some(posix_thread) = self
   115	            .task()
   116	            .as_thread()
   117	            .and_then(|thread| thread.as_posix_thread())
   118	        else {
   119	            return self.wait_until_or_timeout_cancelled(cond, || Ok(()), timeout);
   120	        };
   121	
   122	        let cancel_cond = || {
   123	            if posix_thread.has_pending() {
   124	                return Err(Error::with_message(
   125	                    Errno::EINTR,
   126	                    "the current thread is interrupted by a signal",
   127	                ));
   128	            }
   129	            Ok(())
   130	        };
   131	
   132	        posix_thread.set_signalled_waker(self.waker());
   133	        let res = self.wait_until_or_timeout_cancelled(cond, cancel_cond, timeout);
   134	        posix_thread.clear_signalled_waker();
   135	
   136	        res
   137	    }
   138	
   139	    fn pause_timeout(&self, timeout: &TimeoutExt<'_>) -> Result<()> {
   140	        let timer = timeout.check_expired()?.map(|timeout| {
   141	            let waker = self.waker();
   142	            timeout.create_timer(move || {
   143	                waker.wake_up();
   144	            })
   145	        });
   146	
   147	        let posix_thread_opt = self
   148	            .task()
   149	            .as_thread()
   150	            .and_then(|thread| thread.as_posix_thread());
   151	
   152	        if let Some(posix_thread) = posix_thread_opt {
   153	            posix_thread.set_signalled_waker(self.waker());
   154	            self.wait();
   155	            posix_thread.clear_signalled_waker();
   156	        } else {
   157	            self.wait();
   158	        }
   159	
   160	        if let Some(timer) = timer {
   161	            if timer.remain().is_zero() {
   162	                return_errno_with_message!(Errno::ETIME, "the time limit is reached");
   163	            }
   164	            // If the timeout is not expired, cancel the timer manually.
   165	            timer.cancel();
   166	        }
   167	
   168	        if posix_thread_opt
   169	            .as_ref()
   170	            .is_some_and(|posix_thread| posix_thread.has_pending())
   171	        {
   172	            return_errno_with_message!(
   173	                Errno::EINTR,
   174	                "the current thread is interrupted by a signal"
   175	            );
   176	        }
   177	
   178	        Ok(())
   179	    }
   180	}
   181	
   182	impl Pause for WaitQueue {
   183	    fn pause_until_or_timeout_impl<F, R>(
   184	        &self,
   185	        mut cond: F,
   186	        timeout: Option<&ManagedTimeout>,
   187	    ) -> Result<R>
   188	    where
   189	        F: FnMut() -> Option<R>,
   190	    {
   191	        // Fast path:
   192	        if let Some(res) = cond() {
   193	            return Ok(res);
   194	        }
   195	
   196	        let (waiter, _) = Waiter::new_pair();
   197	        let cond = || {
   198	            self.enqueue(waiter.waker());
   199	            cond()
   200	        };
   201	        waiter.pause_until_or_timeout_impl(cond, timeout)
   202	    }
   203	
   204	    fn pause_timeout(&self, _timeout: &TimeoutExt<'_>) -> Result<()> {
   205	        panic!("`pause_timeout` can only be used on `Waiter`");
   206	    }
   207	}
   208	
   209	/// Executes a closure after temporarily adjusting the signal mask of the current POSIX thread.
   210	pub fn with_sigmask_changed<R>(
   211	    ctx: &Context,
   212	    mask_op: impl FnOnce(SigMask) -> SigMask,
   213	    operate: impl FnOnce() -> R,
   214	) -> R {
   215	    let sig_mask = ctx.posix_thread.sig_mask();
   216	
   217	    // Save the original signal mask and apply the mask updates.
   218	    let old_mask = sig_mask.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
   219	    sig_mask.store(mask_op(old_mask), Ordering::Relaxed);
   220	
   221	    // Perform the operation.
   222	    let res = operate();
   223	
   224	    // Restore the original signal mask.
   225	    sig_mask.store(old_mask, Ordering::Relaxed);
   226	
   227	    res
   228	}

     4	    num::Wrapping,
     5	    sync::atomic::{AtomicBool, Ordering},
     6	};
