     8	use crate::{
     9	    events::IoEvents,
    10	    fs::utils::{Endpoint, EndpointState},
    11	    net::socket::{
    12	        unix::{
    13	            addr::UnixSocketAddrBound, cred::SocketCred, ctrl_msg::AuxiliaryData, UnixSocketAddr,
    14	        },
    15	        util::{options::SocketOptionSet, ControlMessage, SockShutdownCmd},
    16	    },
    17	    prelude::*,

    25	pub(super) struct Connected {
    26	    inner: Endpoint<Inner>,
    27	    reader: Mutex<RbConsumer<u8>>,
    28	    writer: Mutex<RbProducer<u8>>,
    29	    peer_cred: SocketCred,
    30	}
    31	
    32	impl Connected {
    33	    pub(super) fn new_pair(
    34	        addr: Option<UnixSocketAddrBound>,
    35	        peer_addr: Option<UnixSocketAddrBound>,
    36	        state: EndpointState,
    37	        peer_state: EndpointState,
    38	        cred: SocketCred,
    39	        peer_cred: SocketCred,
    40	        options: &SocketOptionSet,
    41	    ) -> (Connected, Connected) {
    42	        let (this_writer, peer_reader) = RingBuffer::new(UNIX_STREAM_DEFAULT_BUF_SIZE).split();
    43	        let (peer_writer, this_reader) = RingBuffer::new(UNIX_STREAM_DEFAULT_BUF_SIZE).split();
    44	
    45	        let this_inner = Inner {
    46	            addr: SpinLock::new(addr),
    47	            state,
    48	            is_pass_cred: AtomicBool::new(options.pass_cred()),
    49	            all_aux: Mutex::new(VecDeque::new()),
    50	            has_aux: AtomicBool::new(false),
    51	        };
    52	        let peer_inner = Inner {
    53	            addr: SpinLock::new(peer_addr),
    54	            state: peer_state,
    55	            is_pass_cred: AtomicBool::new(false),
    56	            all_aux: Mutex::new(VecDeque::new()),
    57	            has_aux: AtomicBool::new(false),
    58	        };
    59	
    60	        let (this_inner, peer_inner) = Endpoint::new_pair(this_inner, peer_inner);
    61	
    62	        let this = Connected {
    63	            inner: this_inner,
    64	            reader: Mutex::new(this_reader),
    65	            writer: Mutex::new(this_writer),
    66	            peer_cred,
    67	        };
    68	        let peer = Connected {
    69	            inner: peer_inner,
    70	            reader: Mutex::new(peer_reader),
    71	            writer: Mutex::new(peer_writer),
    72	            peer_cred: cred,
    73	        };
    74	
    75	        (this, peer)
    76	    }
    77	
    78	    pub(super) fn addr(&self) -> Option<UnixSocketAddrBound> {
    79	        self.inner.this_end().addr.lock().clone()
    80	    }
    81	
    82	    pub(super) fn peer_addr(&self) -> Option<UnixSocketAddrBound> {
    83	        self.inner.peer_end().addr.lock().clone()
    84	    }
    85	
    86	    pub(super) fn bind(&self, addr_to_bind: UnixSocketAddr) -> Result<()> {
    87	        let mut addr = self.inner.this_end().addr.lock();
    88	
    89	        if addr.is_some() {
    90	            return addr_to_bind.bind_unnamed();
    91	        }
    92	
    93	        let bound_addr = addr_to_bind.bind()?;
    94	        *addr = Some(bound_addr);
    95	
    96	        Ok(())
    97	    }
    98	
    99	    pub(super) fn try_read(
   100	        &self,
   101	        writer: &mut dyn MultiWrite,
   102	        is_seqpacket: bool,
   103	    ) -> Result<(usize, Vec<ControlMessage>)> {
   104	        let is_empty = writer.is_empty();
   105	        if is_empty && !is_seqpacket {
   106	            if self.reader.lock().is_empty() {
   107	                return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EAGAIN, "the channel is empty");
   108	            }
   109	            return Ok((0, Vec::new()));
   110	        }
   111	
   112	        let mut reader = self.reader.lock();
   113	        // `reader.len()` is an `Acquire` operation. So it can guarantee that the `has_aux`
   114	        // check below sees the up-to-date value.
   115	        let no_aux_len = reader.len();
   116	
   117	        let peer_end = self.inner.peer_end();
   118	        let is_pass_cred = self.inner.this_end().is_pass_cred.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
   119	
   120	        // Fast path: There are no auxiliary data to receive.
   121	        if !peer_end.has_aux.load(Ordering::Relaxed) {
   122	            let read_len = self
   123	                .inner
   124	                .read_with(move || reader.read_fallible_with_max_len(writer, no_aux_len))?;
   125	            let ctrl_msgs = if is_pass_cred {
   126	                AuxiliaryData::default().generate_control(is_pass_cred)
   127	            } else {
   128	                Vec::new()
   129	            };
   130	            return Ok((read_len, ctrl_msgs));
   131	        }
   132	
   133	        let mut all_aux = peer_end.all_aux.lock();
   134	        let mut aux_prev_data: Option<AuxiliaryData> = None;
   135	        let mut read_tot_len = 0;
   136	
   137	        let aux_data = loop {
   138	            let read_start = reader.head();
   139	            let (aux_len, aux_front) = if let Some(front) = all_aux.front_mut() {
   140	                if front.start == read_start {
   141	                    ((front.end - read_start).0, Some(front))
   142	                } else {
   143	                    ((front.start - read_start).0, None)
   144	                }
   145	            } else {
   146	                (usize::MAX, None)
   147	            };
   148	
   149	            // Unless the auxiliary data we have already received is a subset of the current
   150	            // auxiliary data, we cannot receive additional bytes.
   151	            if let Some(prev) = aux_prev_data.as_mut() {
   152	                let is_subset = if let Some(front) = aux_front.as_ref() {
   153	                    prev.is_subset_of(&front.data, is_pass_cred)
   154	                } else {
   155	                    prev.is_subset_of(&AuxiliaryData::default(), is_pass_cred)
   156	                };
   157	                if !is_subset {
   158	                    break prev;
   159	                }
   160	            }
   161	
   162	            // Read the payload bytes of the current auxiliary data.
   163	            let read_res = if !is_empty && aux_len > 0 {
   164	                self.inner
   165	                    .read_with(|| reader.read_fallible_with_max_len(writer, aux_len))
   166	            } else {
   167	                Ok(0)
   168	            };
   169	            let read_len = match read_res {
   170	                Ok(read_len) => read_len,
   171	                Err(_) if read_tot_len > 0 => break aux_prev_data.as_mut().unwrap(),
   172	                Err(err) => return Err(err),
   173	            };
   174	            read_tot_len += read_len;
   175	
   176	            // Record the current auxiliary data. Break if the read is incomplete or this is a
   177	            // `SOCK_SEQPACKET` socket.
   178	            if is_seqpacket {
   179	                aux_prev_data = Some(all_aux.pop_front().unwrap().data);
   180	                if read_len < aux_len {
   181	                    warn!("setting MSG_TRUNC is not supported");
   182	                    reader.skip(aux_len - read_len);
   183	                }
   184	                break aux_prev_data.as_mut().unwrap();
   185	            } else if let Some(front) = aux_front {
   186	                if read_len < aux_len {
   187	                    front.start += read_len;
   188	                    break &mut front.data;
   189	                }
   190	                aux_prev_data = Some(all_aux.pop_front().unwrap().data);
   191	            } else {
   192	                aux_prev_data = Some(AuxiliaryData::default());
   193	                if read_len < aux_len {
   194	                    break aux_prev_data.as_mut().unwrap();
   195	                }
   196	            }
   197	        };
   198	
   199	        drop(reader);
   200	
   201	        let ctrl_msgs = aux_data.generate_control(is_pass_cred);
   202	        debug_assert!(is_empty || read_tot_len != 0);
   203	        peer_end
   204	            .has_aux
   205	            .store(!all_aux.is_empty(), Ordering::Relaxed);
   206	
   207	        Ok((read_tot_len, ctrl_msgs))
   208	    }
   209	
   210	    pub(super) fn try_write(
   211	        &self,
   212	        reader: &mut dyn MultiRead,
   213	        aux_data: &mut AuxiliaryData,
   214	        is_seqpacket: bool,
   215	    ) -> Result<usize> {
   216	        let is_empty = reader.is_empty();
   217	        if is_empty {
   218	            if self.inner.is_shutdown() {
   219	                return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EPIPE, "the channel is shut down");
   220	            }
   221	            if !is_seqpacket {
   222	                return Ok(0);
   223	            }
   224	        }
   225	
   226	        if is_seqpacket && reader.sum_lens() >= UNIX_STREAM_DEFAULT_BUF_SIZE {
   227	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EMSGSIZE, "the message is too large");
   228	        }
   229	
   230	        let this_end = self.inner.this_end();
   231	        let need_pass_cred = this_end.is_pass_cred.load(Ordering::Relaxed)
   232	            || self.inner.peer_end().is_pass_cred.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
   233	
   234	        // Fast path: There are no auxiliary data to transmit.
   235	        if aux_data.is_empty() && !is_seqpacket && !need_pass_cred {
   236	            let mut writer = self.writer.lock();
   237	            return self.inner.write_with(move || {
   238	                if is_seqpacket && writer.free_len() < reader.sum_lens() {
   239	                    return Ok(0);
   240	                }
   241	                writer.write_fallible(reader)
   242	            });
   243	        }
   244	
   245	        let mut all_aux = this_end.all_aux.lock();
   246	
   247	        // No matter we succeed later or not, set the flag first to ensure that the auxiliary
   248	        // data are always visible to `try_recv`.
   249	        this_end.has_aux.store(true, Ordering::Relaxed);
   250	
   251	        // Write the payload bytes.
   252	        let (write_start, write_res) = if !is_empty {
   253	            let mut writer = self.writer.lock();
   254	            let write_start = writer.tail();
   255	            let write_res = self.inner.write_with(move || {
   256	                if is_seqpacket && writer.free_len() < reader.sum_lens() {
   257	                    return Ok(0);
   258	                }
   259	                writer.write_fallible(reader)
   260	            });
   261	            (write_start, write_res)
   262	        } else {
   263	            (self.writer.lock().tail(), Ok(0))
   264	        };
   265	        let Ok(write_len) = write_res else {
   266	            this_end
   267	                .has_aux
   268	                .store(!all_aux.is_empty(), Ordering::Relaxed);
   269	            return write_res;
   270	        };
   271	
   272	        if need_pass_cred {
   273	            aux_data.fill_cred();
   274	        }
   275	
   276	        // Store the auxiliary data.
   277	        let aux_range = RangedAuxiliaryData {
   278	            data: core::mem::take(aux_data),
   279	            start: write_start,
   280	            end: write_start + Wrapping(write_len),
   281	        };
   282	        all_aux.push_back(aux_range);
   283	
   284	        Ok(write_len)
   285	    }
   286	
   287	    pub(super) fn shutdown(&self, cmd: SockShutdownCmd) {
   288	        if cmd.shut_read() {
   289	            self.inner.peer_shutdown();
   290	        }
   291	
   292	        if cmd.shut_write() {
   293	            self.inner.shutdown();
   294	        }
   295	    }
   296	
   297	    pub(super) fn is_read_shutdown(&self) -> bool {
   298	        self.inner.is_peer_shutdown()
   299	    }
   300	
   301	    pub(super) fn is_write_shutdown(&self) -> bool {
   302	        self.inner.is_shutdown()
   303	    }
   304	
   305	    pub(super) fn set_pass_cred(&self, is_pass_cred: bool) {
   306	        self.inner
   307	            .this_end()
   308	            .is_pass_cred
   309	            .store(is_pass_cred, Ordering::Relaxed);
   310	    }

   312	    pub(super) fn check_io_events(&self) -> IoEvents {

   130	    /// Checks whether the signal can be delivered to the thread.
   131	    ///
   132	    /// For a signal can be delivered to the thread, the sending thread must either
   133	    /// be privileged, or the real or effective user ID of the sending thread must equal
   134	    /// the real or saved set-user-ID of the target thread.
   135	    ///
   136	    /// For SIGCONT, the sending and receiving processes should belong to the same session.
   137	    pub(in crate::process) fn check_signal_perm(
   138	        &self,
   139	        signum: Option<&SigNum>,
   140	        sender: &SignalSenderIds,
   141	    ) -> Result<()> {
   142	        if sender.euid().is_root() {
   143	            return Ok(());
   144	        }
   145	
   146	        if let Some(signum) = signum
   147	            && *signum == SIGCONT
   148	        {
   149	            let receiver_sid = self.process().sid();
   150	            if receiver_sid == sender.sid().unwrap() {
   151	                return Ok(());
   152	            }
   153	
   154	            return_errno_with_message!(
   155	                Errno::EPERM,
   156	                "sigcont requires that sender and receiver belongs to the same session"
   157	            );
   158	        }
   159	
   160	        let (receiver_ruid, receiver_suid) = {
   161	            let credentials = self.credentials();
   162	            (credentials.ruid(), credentials.suid())
   163	        };
   164	
   165	        // FIXME: further check the below code to ensure the behavior is same as Linux. According
   166	        // to man(2) kill, the real or effective user ID of the sending process must equal the
   167	        // real or saved set-user-ID of the target process.
   168	        if sender.ruid() == receiver_ruid
   169	            || sender.ruid() == receiver_suid
   170	            || sender.euid() == receiver_ruid
   171	            || sender.euid() == receiver_suid
   172	        {
   173	            return Ok(());
   174	        }
   175	
   176	        return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EPERM, "sending signal to the thread is not allowed.");
   177	    }

./ostd/src/sync/wait.rs-10-//
./ostd/src/sync/wait.rs-11-// ```

./ostd/src/sync/wait.rs-13-// cond = true;
./ostd/src/sync/wait.rs:14:// wake_up();
./ostd/src/sync/wait.rs-15-//                         wait();

./ostd/src/sync/wait.rs-17-// ```
./ostd/src/sync/wait.rs-18-//

./ostd/src/sync/wait.rs:20:// trivially satisfied if `wake_up()` and `wait()` synchronize with a lock. But if they synchronize
./ostd/src/sync/wait.rs:21:// with an atomic variable, `wake_up()` must access the variable with `Ordering::Release` and
./ostd/src/sync/wait.rs-22-// `wait()` must access the variable with `Ordering::Acquire`.

     1	// SPDX-License-Identifier: MPL-2.0
     2	
     3	use alloc::sync::Arc;
     4	use core::sync::atomic::{AtomicBool, Ordering};
     5	
     6	use crate::{
     7	    events::IoEvents,
     8	    prelude::*,
     9	    process::signal::{PollHandle, Pollee},
    10	};
    11	
    12	/// One of two connected endpoints.
    13	///
    14	/// There is a `T` on the local endpoint and another `T` on the remote endpoint. This type allows
    15	/// users to access the local and remote `T`s from both endpoints.
    16	pub struct Endpoint<T> {
    17	    inner: Arc<Inner<T>>,
    18	    location: Location,
    19	}
    20	
    21	enum Location {
    22	    Client,
    23	    Server,
    24	}
    25	
    26	struct Inner<T> {
    27	    client: T,
    28	    server: T,
    29	}
    30	
    31	impl<T> Endpoint<T> {
    32	    /// Creates an instance pair with two `T`s on the two endpoints.
    33	    ///
    34	    /// For the first instance, `this` is on the local endpoint and `peer` is on the remote
    35	    /// endpoint; for the second instance, `this` is on the remote endpoint and `peer` is on the
    36	    /// local endpoint.
    37	    pub fn new_pair(this: T, peer: T) -> (Endpoint<T>, Endpoint<T>) {
    38	        let inner = Arc::new(Inner {
    39	            client: this,
    40	            server: peer,
    41	        });
    42	
    43	        let client = Endpoint {
    44	            inner: inner.clone(),
    45	            location: Location::Client,
    46	        };
    47	        let server = Endpoint {
    48	            inner,
    49	            location: Location::Server,
    50	        };
    51	
    52	        (client, server)
    53	    }
    54	
    55	    /// Returns a reference to the `T` on the local endpoint.
    56	    pub fn this_end(&self) -> &T {
    57	        match self.location {
    58	            Location::Client => &self.inner.client,
    59	            Location::Server => &self.inner.server,
    60	        }
    61	    }
    62	
    63	    /// Returns a reference to the `T` on the remote endpoint.
    64	    pub fn peer_end(&self) -> &T {
    65	        match self.location {
    66	            Location::Client => &self.inner.server,
    67	            Location::Server => &self.inner.client,
    68	        }
    69	    }
    70	}
    71	
    72	/// An [`Endpoint`] state that helps end-to-end data communication.
    73	///
    74	/// The state contains a [`Pollee`] that will be notified when new data or the buffer becomes
    75	/// available. Additionally, this state tracks whether communication has been shut down, i.e.,
    76	/// whether further data writing is disallowed.
    77	///
    78	/// By having [`EndpointState`] as a part of the endpoint data (i.e., `T` in [`Endpoint`] should
    79	/// implement [`AsRef<EndpointState>`]), methods like [`Endpoint::read_with`],
    80	/// [`Endpoint::write_with`], and [`Endpoint::poll_with`] are available for performing data
    81	/// transmission and registering event observers.
    82	///
    83	/// The data communication can be unidirectional, such as pipes, or bidirectional, such as UNIX
    84	/// sockets.
    85	pub struct EndpointState {
    86	    pollee: Pollee,
    87	    is_shutdown: AtomicBool,
    88	}
    89	
    90	impl EndpointState {
    91	    /// Creates with the [`Pollee`] and the shutdown status.
    92	    pub fn new(pollee: Pollee, is_shutdown: bool) -> Self {
    93	        Self {
    94	            pollee,
    95	            is_shutdown: AtomicBool::new(is_shutdown),
    96	        }
    97	    }
    98	
    99	    /// Clones and returns the [`Pollee`].
   100	    ///
   101	    /// Do not use this method to perform cheap operations on the [`Pollee`] (e.g.,
   102	    /// [`Pollee::notify`]). Use the methods below, such as [`read_with`]/[`write_with`], instead.
   103	    /// This method is deliberately designed to force the [`Pollee`] to be cloned to avoid such
   104	    /// misuse.
   105	    ///
   106	    /// [`read_with`]: Endpoint::read_with
   107	    /// [`write_with`]: Endpoint::read_with
   108	    pub fn cloned_pollee(&self) -> Pollee {
   109	        self.pollee.clone()
   110	    }
   111	}
   112	
   113	impl Default for EndpointState {
   114	    fn default() -> Self {
   115	        Self::new(Pollee::new(), false)
   116	    }
   117	}
   118	
   119	impl AsRef<EndpointState> for EndpointState {
   120	    fn as_ref(&self) -> &EndpointState {
   121	        self
   122	    }
   123	}
   124	
   125	impl<T: AsRef<EndpointState>> Endpoint<T> {
   126	    /// Reads from the endpoint and updates the local/remote [`Pollee`]s.
   127	    ///
   128	    /// Note that if `read` returns `Ok(0)`, it is assumed that there is no data to read and an
   129	    /// [`Errno::EAGAIN`] error will be returned instead.
   130	    ///
   131	    /// However, if the remote endpoint has shut down, this method will return `Ok(0)` to indicate
   132	    /// the end-of-file (EOF).
   133	    pub fn read_with<F>(&self, read: F) -> Result<usize>
   134	    where
   135	        F: FnOnce() -> Result<usize>,
   136	    {
   137	        // This must be recorded before the actual operation to avoid race conditions.
   138	        let is_shutdown = self.is_peer_shutdown();
   139	
   140	        let read_len = read()?;
   141	
   142	        if read_len > 0 {
   143	            self.peer_end().as_ref().pollee.notify(IoEvents::OUT);
   144	            self.this_end().as_ref().pollee.invalidate();
   145	            Ok(read_len)
   146	        } else if is_shutdown {
   147	            Ok(0)
   148	        } else {
   149	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EAGAIN, "the channel is empty");
   150	        }
   151	    }
   152	
   153	    /// Writes to the endpoint and updates the local/remote [`Pollee`]s.
   154	    ///
   155	    /// Note that if `write` returns `Ok(0)`, it is assumed that there is no space to write and an
   156	    /// [`Errno::EAGAIN`] error will be returned instead.
   157	    ///
   158	    /// If the local endpoint has shut down, this method will return an [`Errno::EPIPE`] error
   159	    /// directly without calling the `write` closure.
   160	    pub fn write_with<F>(&self, write: F) -> Result<usize>
   161	    where
   162	        F: FnOnce() -> Result<usize>,
   163	    {
   164	        if self.is_shutdown() {
   165	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EPIPE, "the channel is shut down");
   166	        }
   167	
   168	        let written_len = write()?;
   169	
   170	        if written_len > 0 {
   171	            self.peer_end().as_ref().pollee.notify(IoEvents::IN);
   172	            self.this_end().as_ref().pollee.invalidate();
   173	            Ok(written_len)
   174	        } else {
   175	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EAGAIN, "the channel is full");
   176	        }
   177	    }
   178	
   179	    /// Polls the I/O events in the local [`Pollee`].
   180	    pub fn poll_with<F>(
   181	        &self,
   182	        mask: IoEvents,
   183	        poller: Option<&mut PollHandle>,
   184	        check: F,
   185	    ) -> IoEvents
   186	    where
   187	        F: FnOnce() -> IoEvents,
   188	    {
   189	        self.this_end()
   190	            .as_ref()
   191	            .pollee
   192	            .poll_with(mask, poller, check)
   193	    }
   194	
   195	    /// Shuts down the local endpoint.
   196	    ///
   197	    /// After this method, data cannot be written to from the local endpoint.
   198	    pub fn shutdown(&self) {
   199	        let this_end = self.this_end().as_ref();
   200	        let peer_end = self.peer_end().as_ref();
   201	
   202	        Self::shutdown_impl(this_end, peer_end);
   203	    }
   204	
   205	    /// Shuts down the remote endpoint.
   206	    ///
   207	    /// After this method, data cannot be written to from the remote endpoint.
   208	    pub fn peer_shutdown(&self) {
   209	        let this_end = self.this_end().as_ref();
   210	        let peer_end = self.peer_end().as_ref();
   211	
   212	        Self::shutdown_impl(peer_end, this_end);
   213	    }
   214	
   215	    fn shutdown_impl(this_end: &EndpointState, peer_end: &EndpointState) {
   216	        this_end.is_shutdown.store(true, Ordering::Relaxed);
   217	        peer_end
   218	            .pollee
   219	            .notify(IoEvents::HUP | IoEvents::RDHUP | IoEvents::IN);
   220	        this_end
   221	            .pollee
   222	            .notify(IoEvents::HUP | IoEvents::ERR | IoEvents::OUT);
   223	    }
   224	
   225	    /// Returns whether the local endpoint has shut down.
   226	    pub fn is_shutdown(&self) -> bool {
   227	        self.this_end().as_ref().is_shutdown.load(Ordering::Relaxed)
   228	    }
   229	
   230	    /// Returns whether the remote endpoint has shut down.
   231	    pub fn is_peer_shutdown(&self) -> bool {
   232	        self.peer_end().as_ref().is_shutdown.load(Ordering::Relaxed)
   233	    }
   234	}

./RELEASES.md:3:This release introduces initial support for the **LoongArch CPU architecture**, a major milestone for the project. Version 0.16.0 also significantly expands our Linux ABI compatibility with the addition of **nine new system calls** such as `memfd_create` and `pidfd_open`.
./RELEASES.md:5:Key enhancements include expanded functionality for **UNIX sockets (file descriptor passing and the `SOCK_SEQPACKET` socket type)**, partial support for **netlink sockets of the `NETLINK_KOBJECT_UEVENT` type**, the initial implementation of **CgroupFS**, and a major testing improvement with the integration of system call tests from the **Linux Test Project (LTP)**. We've also adopted **[Nix](https://nix.dev/manual/nix/2.28/introduction)** for building the initramfs, streamlining our cross-compilation and testing workflow.
./RELEASES.md:15:        * [Add the `memfd_create` system call](https://github.com/asterinas/asterinas/pull/2149)
./RELEASES.md:34:        * [Support `SO_PASSCRED` & `SCM_CREDENTIALS` & `SOCK_SEQPACKET` for UNIX sockets](https://github.com/asterinas/asterinas/pull/2268)

     1	# Linux Compatibility
     2	
     3	> "We don't break user space."
     4	>
     5	> --- Linus Torvalds
     6	
     7	Asterinas is dedicated to maintaining compatibility with the Linux ABI,
     8	ensuring that applications and administrative tools
     9	designed for Linux can seamlessly operate within Asterinas.
    10	While we prioritize compatibility,
    11	it is important to note that Asterinas does not,
    12	nor will it in the future,
    13	support the loading of Linux kernel modules.
    14	
    15	## System Calls
    16	
    17	At the time of writing,
    18	Asterinas implements 219 out of the 336 system calls
    19	provided by Linux on x86-64 architecture.

 # Summary
 
-<!-- SUMMARY -->
+The reviewed code keeps the normal signal-aware wait path centralized in `pause_until_or_timeout_impl`, and the Unix socket implementation cleanly tracks payload and auxiliary data separately. The existing workflow also exercises debug, no-KVM, LTP, and gVisor variants in its matrix.
+
+The highest-risk issues are the CI matrix expressions that turn explicit `false` values into `true`, privileged execution of contributor-controlled pull requests, and the Unix seqpacket zero-length path. That path can panic in debug builds, fail to wake readers, and retain unbounded auxiliary metadata. The signal wait path can also miss an already-pending signal, while temporary masks can block `SIGKILL` and `SIGSTOP`; these should be fixed before relying on the affected syscalls. Finally, the shared `Pause` trait exposes an implementation that unconditionally panics and should be narrowed or given a non-panicking behavior.
+
+Documentation and maintainability follow-ups are lower risk: document the supported Unix socket options and seqpacket semantics, add module-level context, replace the mode boolean with an explicit type, and align the trait summary and line wrapping with the repository guidelines.
 
 ## Maintainability
 
@@ -123,7 +127,7 @@
 
 Lost wakeup (major): `pause_timeout` checks `has_pending()` only after `self.wait()`. If a signal is already pending before `set_signalled_waker()` runs, no wakeup is generated for this waiter; with `TimeoutExt::Never` the thread sleeps indefinitely, and with a finite timeout it waits until expiry before returning `EINTR`.
 
-**Fix.** Check `posix_thread.has_pending()` before sleeping and keep the pending-signal check in the same cancellation loop used by `pause_until_or_timeout_cancelled`.
+**Fix.** Shared with Security `kernel/src/process/signal/pause.rs` line 153: check `posix_thread.has_pending()` after registering the waker and before sleeping, or route this method through the same cancellation loop used by `pause_until_or_timeout_cancelled`.
 
 ### `kernel/src/process/signal/pause.rs` line 205
 
@@ -135,7 +139,7 @@
 
 Reachable panic (minor): `WaitQueue::pause_timeout()` is callable through the public `Pause` trait but unconditionally invokes `panic!`, so a safe caller can trigger a kernel panic by calling the method on a `WaitQueue`.
 
-**Fix.** Remove `pause_timeout` from the shared trait and expose it only for `Waiter`, or return an ordinary error such as `EOPNOTSUPP` instead of panicking.
+**Fix.** Shared with Maintainability `kernel/src/process/signal/pause.rs` line 204: remove `pause_timeout` from the shared trait and expose it only for `Waiter`, or return an ordinary error such as `EOPNOTSUPP` instead of panicking.
 
 ## Security
 
@@ -183,7 +187,7 @@
 
 Lost wake-up (major): `pause_timeout()` does not check `posix_thread.has_pending()` after installing the waker and before calling `self.wait()`. If a deliverable signal is already pending when this path starts, installing the waker does not generate a wake-up, so an infinite-timeout `poll()` or `ppoll()` can sleep indefinitely despite the signal.
 
-**Fix.** Check `posix_thread.has_pending()` after registering the waker and before waiting, or use the cancellation loop from `pause_until_or_timeout_impl` so already-pending signals cannot be missed.
+**Fix.** Shared with Correctness `kernel/src/process/signal/pause.rs` line 154: check `posix_thread.has_pending()` after registering the waker and before waiting, or use the cancellation loop from `pause_until_or_timeout_impl` so already-pending signals cannot be missed.

+**Fix.** Shared with Security `kernel/src/process/signal/pause.rs` line 153: check `posix_thread.has_pending()` after registering the waker and before sleeping, or route this method through the same cancellation loop used by `pause_until_or_timeout_cancelled`.

# Summary

The reviewed code keeps the normal signal-aware wait path centralized in `pause_until_or_timeout_impl`, and the Unix socket implementation cleanly tracks payload and auxiliary data separately. The existing workflow also exercises debug, no-KVM, LTP, and gVisor variants in its matrix.

The highest-risk issues are the CI matrix expressions that turn explicit `false` values into `true`, privileged execution of contributor-controlled pull requests, and the Unix seqpacket zero-length path. That path can panic in debug builds, fail to wake readers, and retain unbounded auxiliary metadata. The signal wait path can also miss an already-pending signal, while temporary masks can block `SIGKILL` and `SIGSTOP`; these should be fixed before relying on the affected syscalls. Finally, the shared `Pause` trait exposes an implementation that unconditionally panics and should be narrowed or given a non-panicking behavior.

Documentation and maintainability follow-ups are lower risk: document the supported Unix socket options and seqpacket semantics, add module-level context, replace the mode boolean with an explicit type, and align the trait summary and line wrapping with the repository guidelines.

## Maintainability

### `kernel/src/net/socket/unix/stream/connected.rs` line 1

> ```diff
> // SPDX-License-Identifier: MPL-2.0
> 
> use core::{
> ```

`module-docs` (minor): `connected.rs` defines the connected Unix stream socket state, buffers, auxiliary-data tracking, and peer credentials, but the module has no `//!` overview. Readers have to infer how `Connected` relates to `socket.rs`, `init.rs`, and `listener.rs` from the implementation.

**Fix.** Add a short `//!` module doc after the SPDX line that describes the connected-state role, the key `Connected` type, and how it is entered from the surrounding Unix stream socket modules.

### `kernel/src/net/socket/unix/stream/connected.rs` line 99

### `kernel/src/net/socket/unix/stream/connected.rs` line 48

> ```diff
>             is_pass_cred: AtomicBool::new(options.pass_cred()),
> ```

`linux-compat-docs` (major): Initializing `is_pass_cred` from `SocketOptionSet::pass_cred()` enables user-visible `SO_PASSCRED` and `SCM_CREDENTIALS` behavior for `sendmsg(2)` and `recvmsg(2)`, but the Linux Compatibility documentation has no corresponding flag, option, or ancillary-data coverage.

**Fix.** Update the Linux Compatibility syscall coverage page and its `.scml` file for `setsockopt(2)`, `sendmsg(2)`, and `recvmsg(2)`, documenting `SO_PASSCRED` and `SCM_CREDENTIALS`.

### `kernel/src/net/socket/unix/stream/connected.rs` line 178

> ```diff
>             // Record the current auxiliary data. Break if the read is incomplete or this is a
>             // `SOCK_SEQPACKET` socket.
> ```

`linux-compat-docs` (major): The `is_seqpacket` branch provides user-visible `SOCK_SEQPACKET` packet-receive behavior, but the Linux Compatibility documentation only marks `socket(2)`, `socketpair(2)`, and `recvmsg(2)` as implemented without documenting this supported behavior or the `MSG_TRUNC` limitation.

**Fix.** Update the Linux Compatibility syscall coverage page and its `.scml` file for `socket(2)`, `socketpair(2)`, and `recvmsg(2)`, including `SOCK_SEQPACKET` semantics and the unsupported `MSG_TRUNC` behavior.

### `kernel/src/process/signal/pause.rs` line 31

> ```diff
>     /// This method will return an error with [`EINTR`] if a signal is received before the
>     /// condition is met.
> ```

`semantic-line-breaks` (nit): The `# Errors` sentence wraps after the article `the`, leaving the line break in the middle of a clause; the surrounding doc comments use the same arbitrary wrapping.

**Fix.** Break the prose at the clause boundary:
```rust
/// This method will return an error with [`EINTR`]
/// if a signal is received before the condition is met.
```
